````python
    opts = dict(number_check(minimum, maximum, decimal))
    if note:
        opts["input_message"] = note
    return opts


def blank(trigger: str, body: str) -> str:
    """`=IF(trigger="","",body)` with the standard row placeholders."""
    return f'=IF({{{trigger}}}{{r}}="","",{body})'


def zero(trigger: str, body: str) -> str:
    """`=IF(trigger="",0,body)` - for columns that must never return "".

    Any column a `SUMPRODUCT`, `MAX(cond*range)` or calendar lookup reads
    has to be numeric on every single row, including the ones that do not
    apply, or Excel multiplies a number by an empty string and answers
    `#VALUE!`.
    """
    return f'=IF({{{trigger}}}{{r}}="",0,{body})'


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Cross sheet lookups.  Every one is blank-row safe and says what to do
# when the ID does not exist, because a typo in an ID is the single most
# common thing that goes wrong in a workbook like this.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def lookup(sheet: str, target: str, key_col: str, key: str,
           missing: str = "") -> str:
    """Generic `INDEX/MATCH` against any register, blank row safe."""
    return (f'=IF({{{key}}}{{r}}="","",IFERROR(INDEX([[{sheet}.'
            f'{target}]],MATCH({{{key}}}{{r}},'
            f'[[{sheet}.{key_col}]],0)),"{missing}"))')


def lookup_num(sheet: str, target: str, key_col: str, key: str) -> str:
    """Numeric `INDEX/MATCH` - 0 when the ID is missing or the cell empty.

    `INDEX` on an empty cell returns 0, not "", so numeric lookups are
    coerced with `*1` inside the `IFERROR` rather than compared to "".
    """
    return (f'=IF({{{key}}}{{r}}="","",IFERROR(INDEX([[{sheet}.'
            f'{target}]],MATCH({{{key}}}{{r}},'
            f'[[{sheet}.{key_col}]],0))*1,0))')


def lookup_num_zero(sheet: str, target: str, key_col: str, key: str) -> str:
    """As :func:`lookup_num`, but returns 0 rather than "" on a blank row."""
    return (f'=IF({{{key}}}{{r}}="",0,IFERROR(INDEX([[{sheet}.'
            f'{target}]],MATCH({{{key}}}{{r}},'
            f'[[{sheet}.{key_col}]],0))*1,0))')

def rank_desc(key: str, guard: str = '{name}{r}=""',
              header: str = "Rank", width: float = 9,
              note: str = "") -> Col:
    """Hidden dense rank, 1 = the biggest value in `key`.

    Ties are broken by row order, so the ranks come out 1, 2, 3 ... with
    no gaps and no duplicates.  Pages then read the nth row with an
    ordinary `MATCH(n, rank, 0)` - no array formula, so nothing in the
    workbook needs Ctrl+Shift+Enter.
    """
    return Col(f"{key}_rank", header, width, "int",
               formula=(f'=IF({guard},"",COUNTIFS({{{key}_first}}:'
                        f'{{{key}_last}},">"&{{{key}}}{{r}})+'
                        f'COUNTIFS({{{key}_first}}:{{{key}}}{{r}},'
                        f'{{{key}}}{{r}}))'),
               hidden=True, note=note or f"1 = the largest {key}.")


def rank_asc(key: str, guard: str = '{name}{r}=""',
             header: str = "Rank", width: float = 9,
             note: str = "") -> Col:
    """Hidden dense rank, 1 = the smallest value in `key`."""
    return Col(f"{key}_rank", header, width, "int",
               formula=(f'=IF({guard},"",COUNTIFS({{{key}_first}}:'
                        f'{{{key}_last}},"<"&{{{key}}}{{r}})+'
                        f'COUNTIFS({{{key}_first}}:{{{key}}}{{r}},'
                        f'{{{key}}}{{r}}))'),
               hidden=True, note=note or f"1 = the smallest {key}.")


def seq_key(parent: str, trigger: str = "") -> List[Col]:
    """Hidden `1, 2, 3 ...` within a parent, plus a `parent|n` key.

    Printables and per-parent summaries use `MATCH(parent & "|" & n, key, 0)`
    to pull the nth line of one record without an array formula.
    """
    trig = trigger or parent
    return [
        Col("seq", "Line", 6, "int",
            formula=blank(
                trig,
                f'COUNTIFS({{{parent}_first}}:{{{parent}}}{{r}},'
                f'{{{parent}}}{{r}})'),
            hidden=True),
        Col("key", "Key", 14, "text",
            formula=blank(trig, f'{{{parent}}}{{r}}&"|"&{{seq}}{{r}}'),
            hidden=True),
    ]


def days_until(date_cell: str) -> str:
    """Whole days from today to a date - negative once it has passed."""
    return f'({date_cell}-TodayDate)'


def countdown_band(days_cell: str) -> str:
    """Turn a day count into the band the dashboard groups on."""
    return (f'IF({days_cell}<0,"Past",'
            f'IF({days_cell}<=7,"This week",'
            f'IF({days_cell}<=30,"Next 30 days",'
            f'IF({days_cell}<=90,"1-3 months",'
            f'IF({days_cell}<=180,"3-6 months",'
            f'IF({days_cell}<=365,"6-12 months","Over a year"))))))')


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Cross sheet lookups - the four join keys
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# A Customer ID owns Property IDs. A Property ID owns Jobs. A Job owns
# its labour, its materials and its line on an invoice. Every one of
# those IDs is a drop-down somewhere, never a typed string, because a
# mistyped ID is a row that silently stops being part of the business.

def lookup_customer(target: str, key: str = "customer",
                    missing: str = "Not in Customer CRM") -> str:
    """A field from the customer record behind a Customer ID."""
    return lookup("Customer CRM", target, "id", key, missing)


def lookup_customer_num(target: str, key: str = "customer") -> str:
    return lookup_num("Customer CRM", target, "id", key)


def lookup_property(target: str, key: str = "property",
                    missing: str = "Not in Property Database") -> str:
    """A field from the property record behind a Property ID."""
    return lookup("Property Database", target, "id", key, missing)


def lookup_property_num(target: str, key: str = "property") -> str:
    return lookup_num("Property Database", target, "id", key)


def lookup_property_num_zero(target: str, key: str = "property") -> str:
    return lookup_num_zero("Property Database", target, "id", key)


def lookup_service(target: str, key: str = "service",
                   missing: str = "Not in Service Catalog") -> str:
    """A field from the service record behind a service name."""
    return lookup("Service Catalog", target, "name", key, missing)


def lookup_service_num(target: str, key: str = "service") -> str:
    return lookup_num("Service Catalog", target, "name", key)


def lookup_service_num_zero(target: str, key: str = "service") -> str:
    return lookup_num_zero("Service Catalog", target, "name", key)


def lookup_job(target: str, key: str = "job",
               missing: str = "Not in Job & Work Orders") -> str:
    """A field from the job record behind a Job ID."""
    return lookup("Job & Work Orders", target, "id", key, missing)


def lookup_job_num(target: str, key: str = "job") -> str:
    return lookup_num("Job & Work Orders", target, "id", key)


def lookup_crew(target: str, key: str = "employee",
                missing: str = "Not in Crew Database") -> str:
    """A field from the employee record behind an Employee ID."""
    return lookup("Crew Database", target, "id", key, missing)


def lookup_crew_num(target: str, key: str = "employee") -> str:
    return lookup_num("Crew Database", target, "id", key)


def lookup_contract(target: str, key: str = "contract",
                    missing: str = "") -> str:
    return lookup("Recurring Contracts", target, "id", key, missing)


def lookup_invoice_num(target: str, key: str = "invoice") -> str:
    return lookup_num("Invoice & Payments", target, "id", key)


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# The standard "who and where" columns
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def customer_col(key: str = "customer", header: str = "Customer",
                 width: float = 11, note: str = "") -> Col:
    """A Customer ID chosen from the live list, never typed."""
    return Col(key, header, width, "id",
               validate=pick(
                   "CustomerList",
                   "Pick the Customer ID. Add the customer on the "
                   "Customer CRM tab first if they are not listed."),
               note=note or "Pick from the list. A typed ID disconnects "
                            "this row from the customer.")


def property_col(key: str = "property", header: str = "Property",
                 width: float = 11, note: str = "") -> Col:
    return Col(key, header, width, "id",
               validate=pick(
                   "PropertyList",
                   "Pick the Property ID. Add it on the Property "
                   "Database tab first if it is not listed."),
               note=note or "Pick from the list. One customer can own "
                            "several properties.")


def service_col(key: str = "service", header: str = "Service",
                width: float = 24, note: str = "") -> Col:
    return Col(key, header, width, "text",
               validate=pick(
                   "ServiceList",
                   "Pick the service. Add it to the Service Catalog "
                   "tab first if it is not listed."),
               note=note or "Drives the default price and the time "
                            "estimate.")


def customer_name_col(key: str = "customer", width: float = 22) -> Col:
    return Col("customer_name", "Customer name", width, "text",
               formula=lookup_customer("name", key),
               note="Automatic. If it says 'Not in Customer CRM' the ID "
                    "is wrong.")


def property_address_col(key: str = "property", width: float = 30) -> Col:
    return Col("address", "Address", width, "text",
               formula=lookup_property("address", key),
               note="Automatic, from the Property Database.")


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Status set tests - what a status MEANS, defined once
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def in_list(cell: str, values) -> str:
    """`(c="a")+(c="b")>0` - membership without OR().

    OR() evaluates every argument, so a blank cell in an argument that
    an earlier test should have ruled out still reaches the comparison.
    Adding booleans avoids it and is shorter in the formula bar.
    """
    terms = "+".join(f'({cell}="{value}")' for value in values)
    return f"({terms}>0)"


def is_open_job(cell: str) -> str:
    """A job that still has to happen."""
    return in_list(cell, OPEN_JOB_STATUSES)


def is_done_job(cell: str) -> str:
    """A job that was done - the only kind that earns anything."""
    return in_list(cell, DONE_JOB_STATUSES)


def is_catchup_job(cell: str) -> str:
    """Rained off or needing a revisit: work you still owe."""
    return in_list(cell, CATCHUP_JOB_STATUSES)


def is_won_quote(cell: str) -> str:
    return in_list(cell, WON_QUOTE_STATUSES)


def is_live_quote(cell: str) -> str:
    """Out with the customer and not yet answered."""
    return in_list(cell, LIVE_QUOTE_STATUSES)


def is_owed_invoice(cell: str) -> str:
    """Money that is still yours and not yet in the bank."""
    return in_list(cell, OWED_INVOICE_STATUSES)


def is_paid_invoice(cell: str) -> str:
    return in_list(cell, PAID_INVOICE_STATUSES)


def is_live_contract(cell: str) -> str:
    return in_list(cell, LIVE_CONTRACT_STATUSES)


def is_active_customer(cell: str) -> str:
    return in_list(cell, ACTIVE_CUSTOMER_STATUSES)


def is_usable_equipment(cell: str) -> str:
    return in_list(cell, USABLE_EQUIPMENT_STATUSES)


def is_job_cost(cell: str) -> str:
    """An expense that belongs to a job, not to the business."""
    return in_list(cell, JOB_COST_CATEGORIES)


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Roll-ups across the registers
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def sum_by(sheet: str, value_col: str, key_col: str, key_cell: str,
           *extra: str) -> str:
    """`SUMIFS` over one register, keyed on an ID.

    `extra` arrives in pairs - a column key and a criterion - because a
    SUMIFS criterion is always TWO arguments and writing it as one is
    the fastest way to a #VALUE! nobody can find.
    """
    parts = [f"[[{sheet}.{value_col}]]", f"[[{sheet}.{key_col}]]", key_cell]
    for index in range(0, len(extra), 2):
        parts.append(f"[[{sheet}.{extra[index]}]]")
        parts.append(extra[index + 1])
    return f"SUMIFS({','.join(parts)})"


def count_by(sheet: str, key_col: str, key_cell: str, *extra: str) -> str:
    parts = [f"[[{sheet}.{key_col}]]", key_cell]
    for index in range(0, len(extra), 2):
        parts.append(f"[[{sheet}.{extra[index]}]]")
        parts.append(extra[index + 1])
    return f"COUNTIFS({','.join(parts)})"


def jobs_done(key_col: str, key_cell: str) -> str:
    """Completed visits against a customer, property or service."""
    return count_by("Job & Work Orders", key_col, key_cell, "is_done", "1")


def revenue_from_jobs(key_col: str, key_cell: str) -> str:
    """What completed work was worth, before tax."""
    return sum_by("Job & Work Orders", "charge", key_col, key_cell,
                  "is_done", "1")


def labour_hours(job_cell: str) -> str:
    """Hours actually booked to a job on the timesheets."""
    return sum_by("Timesheets & Payroll", "hours", "job", job_cell)


def labour_cost(job_cell: str) -> str:
    """What those hours cost, at each person's own rate."""
    return sum_by("Timesheets & Payroll", "cost", "job", job_cell)


def material_cost(job_cell: str) -> str:
    """Materials booked out to a job, at cost."""
    return sum_by("Expense Tracker", "amount", "job", job_cell,
                  "is_job_cost", "1")


def invoiced_amount(job_cell: str) -> str:
    return sum_by("Invoice & Payments", "total", "job", job_cell)


def paid_amount(invoice_cell: str) -> str:
    return sum_by("Invoice & Payments", "paid", "id", invoice_cell)


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# The money
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def quote_price(area_cell: str, service_cell: str) -> str:
    """The catalogue price for a service at a given lawn area.

    Everything in the Service Catalog is priced per unit, and the unit
    varies - per visit, per hour, per 1,000 sq ft, per linear foot. The
    catalogue carries the rate and the unit; this works out which
    multiplier applies so one formula covers all thirty services.
    """
    rate = f'IFERROR(INDEX([[Service Catalog.rate]],MATCH({service_cell},[[Service Catalog.name]],0))*1,0)'
    unit = f'IFERROR(INDEX([[Service Catalog.unit]],MATCH({service_cell},[[Service Catalog.name]],0))&"","")'
    return (f'IF({service_cell}="",0,'
            f'ROUND({rate}*'
            f'IF({unit}="Per 1,000 sq ft",{area_cell}/1000,'
            f'IF({unit}="Per sq ft",{area_cell},'
            f'IF({unit}="Per 100 sq ft",{area_cell}/100,1)))'
            ',2))')


def service_minutes(area_cell: str, service_cell: str) -> str:
    """How long the catalogue says this service takes on this lawn."""
    mins = f'IFERROR(INDEX([[Service Catalog.minutes]],MATCH({service_cell},[[Service Catalog.name]],0))*1,0)'
    unit = f'IFERROR(INDEX([[Service Catalog.unit]],MATCH({service_cell},[[Service Catalog.name]],0))&"","")'
    return (f'IF({service_cell}="",0,'
            f'ROUND({mins}*'
            f'IF({unit}="Per 1,000 sq ft",{area_cell}/1000,'
            f'IF({unit}="Per sq ft",{area_cell},'
            f'IF({unit}="Per 100 sq ft",{area_cell}/100,1)))'
            ',0))')


def travel_cost(miles_cell: str) -> str:
    """What getting there costs the job."""
    return f'ROUND({miles_cell}*TravelRate,2)'


def overhead_cost(hours_cell: str) -> str:
    """The yard, the insurance and the admin, per crew hour.

    Leaving this out is how a job that looks profitable turns out not
    to be: a three-hour visit carries three hours of the business
    whether or not anybody thought about it.
    """
    return f'ROUND({hours_cell}*OverheadRate,2)'


def service_tax(amount: str) -> str:
    """Sales tax or VAT, only if the business is registered for it."""
    return f'IF(TaxOnServices<>"Yes",0,ROUND({amount}*TaxRate,2))'


def overtime_split(hours_cell: str) -> str:
    """Hours over the weekly threshold, which cost more."""
    return f'MAX(0,{hours_cell}-OvertimeAfter)'


def margin(part: str, whole: str) -> str:
    """A percentage that is blank rather than wrong when there is no base."""
    return f'IF({whole}<=0,"",ROUND({part}/{whole},4))'


def visits_per_year(frequency_cell: str) -> str:
    """Turn 'Fortnightly' into 26 - the parallel list does the work."""
    return ('IFERROR(INDEX(VisitsPerYear,'
            f'MATCH({frequency_cell},FrequencyList,0))*1,0)')


def days_between_visits(frequency_cell: str) -> str:
    return ('IFERROR(INDEX(DaysBetween,'
            f'MATCH({frequency_cell},FrequencyList,0))*1,0)')


def term_days(terms_cell: str) -> str:
    return f'IFERROR(INDEX(TermDays,MATCH({terms_cell},PaymentTermsList,0)),0)'


def season_of(date_cell: str) -> str:
    """Which season a date falls in, for the seasonal roll-ups."""
    return (f'IF({date_cell}="","",'
            f'IF(MONTH({date_cell})<=2,"Winter",'
            f'IF(MONTH({date_cell})<=5,"Spring",'
            f'IF(MONTH({date_cell})<=8,"Summer",'
            f'IF(MONTH({date_cell})<=11,"Autumn / Fall","Winter")))))')


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Alerts
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def alert_chain(trigger: str, tests, default: str = "OK") -> str:
    """Nested IFs from `(condition, message)` pairs, most urgent first.

    Nested rather than OR() or IFS(): OR() evaluates every argument
    even after one is already true, and IFS() needs a modern Excel.
    """
    body = f'"{default}"'
    for condition, message in reversed(tests):
        body = f'IF({condition},"{message}",{body})'
    return f'=IF({{{trigger}}}{{r}}="","",{body})'
````

---

## 4. Settings and the two front pages

Settings & Lists is the only tab a buyer has to fill in before anything else works: the business, the labour and charge-out rates, the overhead per crew hour, the target and floor margins, the minimum visit, the travel rate, the service intervals, the season, the targets and 44 drop-down lists. The Dashboard is the centrepiece - today's round, the week ahead, the work that slipped, the money owed, machines due a service, materials running out, and a matrix that counts every alert on sixteen registers. Getting Started explains the four IDs and is honest about what the workbook is not.

### `landscaping_spreadsheet/sheets/settings.py`

*599 lines · 2 functions*

> `Settings & Lists` - every number and every list the workbook runs on.
>
> Nothing here is hard coded to one country, one currency or one way of
> running a lawn care round. Your crew rate, your charge-out rate, your
> minimum visit, your travel rate, your margin floor, your service
> intervals and all fifty-odd drop-downs are set here, once, and every
> other tab follows.
>
> Three groups deserve a second look before you start.
>
> The crew cost rate should be the loaded cost - wages plus payroll tax
> plus insurance - not what lands in somebody's bank account. Put the
> take-home figure in and every job in this workbook will look more
> profitable than it is.
>
> The overhead per crew hour is the one most people leave at zero. The
> yard, the truck insurance, the phone, the accountant and the evenings
> doing paperwork are all real, and a three-hour visit carries three
> hours of them whether or not anybody works it out.
>
> The ID prefixes decide what your Customer IDs, Property IDs, Job IDs
> and Invoice IDs look like. Change them before you have typed two
> hundred customers, not after.

| Defines | What it does |
|---------|--------------|
| `_month_keys(today, back, ahead)` | `yyyy-mm` keys around today - what every monthly total groups on |
| `build(book, fmts, cfg, reg)` |  |

**Module constants:** `NAME` ('Settings & Lists'), `LAST_COL` (11), `LISTS` (44 items).

````python
"""`Settings & Lists` - every number and every list the workbook runs on.

Nothing here is hard coded to one country, one currency or one way of
running a lawn care round. Your crew rate, your charge-out rate, your
minimum visit, your travel rate, your margin floor, your service
intervals and all fifty-odd drop-downs are set here, once, and every
other tab follows.

Three groups deserve a second look before you start.

The crew cost rate should be the loaded cost - wages plus payroll tax
plus insurance - not what lands in somebody's bank account. Put the
take-home figure in and every job in this workbook will look more
profitable than it is.

The overhead per crew hour is the one most people leave at zero. The
yard, the truck insurance, the phone, the accountant and the evenings
doing paperwork are all real, and a three-hour visit carries three
hours of them whether or not anybody works it out.

The ID prefixes decide what your Customer IDs, Property IDs, Job IDs
and Invoice IDs look like. Change them before you have typed two
hundred customers, not after.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import datetime as _dt
from typing import List, Tuple

from ..config import (ABSENCE_REASONS, ACCESS_TYPES, CONTRACT_STATUSES,
                      CREW_NAMES, CREW_ROLES, CREW_STATUSES, CURRENCIES,
                      CUSTOMER_STATUSES, CUSTOMER_TYPES, DEFAULT_VEHICLES,
                      DISCLAIMER, EMPLOYMENT_TYPES, EQUIPMENT_CATEGORIES,
                      EQUIPMENT_CONDITIONS, EQUIPMENT_STATUSES,
                      EXPENSE_CATEGORIES, FEEDBACK_CAUSES,
                      FEEDBACK_SOURCES, FEEDBACK_STATUSES, FEEDBACK_TYPES,
                      FREQUENCIES, HONESTY_NOTE, ID_PREFIX_FIELDS,
                      INVOICE_STATUSES, JOB_STATUSES, LEAD_SOURCES,
                      MAINTENANCE_TYPES, MATERIAL_CATEGORIES,
                      MATERIAL_STATUSES, MATERIAL_UNITS, MONTH_NAMES,
                      PAYMENT_METHODS, PAYMENT_TERMS, PAYROLL_NOTE,
                      PHOTO_NOTE, PRICING_UNITS, PRIORITIES, PRIVACY_NOTE,
                      PROPERTY_STATUSES, PROPERTY_TYPES, QUOTE_STATUSES,
                      ROUTE_NOTE, SEASONS, SERVICE_CATEGORIES,
                      SERVICE_STATUSES, STOCK_MOVES, TASK_TYPES,
                      TAX_CATEGORIES, TERRAIN_TYPES, VEHICLE_COST_TYPES,
                      WEATHER_CONDITIONS, WEATHER_NOTE, WEEKDAYS, YES_NO,
                      Config, Palette)
from ..layout import Registry, col_letter, dynamic_name, quote_sheet
from ..writer import SheetWriter, list_from, number_check, yes_no

NAME = "Settings & Lists"
LAST_COL = 11

#: name -> values. Every drop-down in the workbook comes from this table.
LISTS: List[Tuple[str, List[str]]] = [
    ("YesNoList", list(YES_NO)),
    ("CustomerTypeList", list(CUSTOMER_TYPES)),
    ("CustomerStatusList", list(CUSTOMER_STATUSES)),
    ("LeadSourceList", list(LEAD_SOURCES)),
    ("PropertyTypeList", list(PROPERTY_TYPES)),
    ("PropertyStatusList", list(PROPERTY_STATUSES)),
    ("TerrainList", list(TERRAIN_TYPES)),
    ("AccessList", list(ACCESS_TYPES)),
    ("ServiceCategoryList", list(SERVICE_CATEGORIES)),
    ("ServiceStatusList", list(SERVICE_STATUSES)),
    ("PricingUnitList", list(PRICING_UNITS)),
    ("QuoteStatusList", list(QUOTE_STATUSES)),
    ("JobStatusList", list(JOB_STATUSES)),
    ("PriorityList", list(PRIORITIES)),
    ("WeatherList", list(WEATHER_CONDITIONS)),
    ("ContractStatusList", list(CONTRACT_STATUSES)),
    ("CrewNameList", list(CREW_NAMES)),
    ("CrewRoleList", list(CREW_ROLES)),
    ("CrewStatusList", list(CREW_STATUSES)),
    ("EmploymentTypeList", list(EMPLOYMENT_TYPES)),
    ("AbsenceList", list(ABSENCE_REASONS)),
    ("TaskTypeList", list(TASK_TYPES)),
    ("EquipmentCategoryList", list(EQUIPMENT_CATEGORIES)),
    ("EquipmentStatusList", list(EQUIPMENT_STATUSES)),
    ("EquipmentConditionList", list(EQUIPMENT_CONDITIONS)),
    ("MaintenanceTypeList", list(MAINTENANCE_TYPES)),
    ("VehicleList", list(DEFAULT_VEHICLES)),
    ("VehicleCostList", list(VEHICLE_COST_TYPES)),
    ("MaterialCategoryList", list(MATERIAL_CATEGORIES)),
    ("MaterialUnitList", list(MATERIAL_UNITS)),
    ("MaterialStatusList", list(MATERIAL_STATUSES)),
    ("StockMoveList", list(STOCK_MOVES)),
    ("InvoiceStatusList", list(INVOICE_STATUSES)),
    ("PaymentMethodList", list(PAYMENT_METHODS)),
    ("ExpenseCategoryList", list(EXPENSE_CATEGORIES)),
    ("TaxCategoryList", list(TAX_CATEGORIES)),
    ("FeedbackSourceList", list(FEEDBACK_SOURCES)),
    ("FeedbackTypeList", list(FEEDBACK_TYPES)),
    ("FeedbackStatusList", list(FEEDBACK_STATUSES)),
    ("FeedbackCauseList", list(FEEDBACK_CAUSES)),
    ("SeasonList", list(SEASONS)),
    ("WeekdayList", list(WEEKDAYS)),
    ("MonthNameList", list(MONTH_NAMES)),
    ("CurrencyList", list(CURRENCIES)),
]


def _month_keys(today: _dt.date, back: int = 18,
                ahead: int = 18) -> List[str]:
    """`yyyy-mm` keys around today - what every monthly total groups on."""
    start = today.year * 12 + today.month - 1 - back
    return [f"{(start + n) // 12:04d}-{(start + n) % 12 + 1:02d}"
            for n in range(back + ahead + 1)]


def build(book, fmts, cfg: Config, reg: Registry) -> SheetWriter:
    s = SheetWriter(book, fmts, cfg, reg, NAME, tab_color=Palette.ink)
    sheet_ref = quote_sheet(NAME)

    s.banner("Settings & Lists",
             "Your business, your rates, your rules, your lists. Set "
             "these once and all twenty-three tabs follow.",
             LAST_COL)
    s.set_col(0, 0, 3)
    s.set_col(1, 2, 30)
    s.set_col(3, 3, 18)
    s.set_col(4, LAST_COL, 17)

    row = 4
    row = s.note(
        row,
        "White cells are yours to change; everything else is locked "
        "and calculated. Nothing here assumes a country, a currency "
        "or a size of business - put your own rates, your own "
        "margins and your own service intervals in, and the rest of "
        "the workbook follows.",
        LAST_COL, height=34)
    row += 1

    # ------------------------------------------------------ business --
    row = s.section(row, "Your business", LAST_COL)
    row = s.field(row, "Business name", cfg.business.name,
                  name="BusinessName", value_end=5, help_col=7,
                  help_text="Printed at the top of every quote, "
                            "invoice and crew work order.")
    row = s.field(row, "Owner", cfg.business.owner, name="OwnerName",
                  value_end=5, help_col=7)
    row = s.field(row, "Email", cfg.business.email,
                  name="BusinessEmail", value_end=5, help_col=7)
    row = s.field(row, "Phone", cfg.business.phone,
                  name="BusinessPhone", value_end=5, help_col=7)
    row = s.field(row, "Website", cfg.business.website, value_end=5,
                  help_col=7)
    row = s.field(row, "Yard / depot address", cfg.business.address_1,
                  name="BusinessAddress", value_end=5, help_col=7,
                  help_text="Where the trucks start from. Travel "
                            "distances on the Property Database are "
                            "measured from here.")
    row = s.field(row, "Address line 2", cfg.business.address_2,
                  value_end=5, help_col=7)
    row = s.field(row, "City", cfg.business.city, name="BusinessCity",
                  value_end=5, help_col=7)
    row = s.field(row, "Region / state", cfg.business.region,
                  value_end=5, help_col=7)
    row = s.field(row, "Zip / postcode", cfg.business.postcode)
    row = s.field(row, "Country", cfg.business.country, value_end=5,
                  help_col=7)
    row = s.field(row, "Tax / VAT number", cfg.business.tax_id,
                  value_end=5, help_col=7)
    row = s.field(row, "Licence number", cfg.business.licence,
                  name="BusinessLicence", value_end=5, help_col=7,
                  help_text="Pesticide applicator or contractor "
                            "licence, if your state or country "
                            "requires one on the paperwork.")
    row = s.field(row, "Insurance policy", cfg.business.insurance,
                  value_end=5, help_col=7,
                  help_text="Liability policy number. Commercial "
                            "customers ask for it.")
    row = s.field(row, "Currency symbol", cfg.currency,
                  name="CurrencySign",
                  help_text="Shown on screen. The cell formats "
                            "follow the symbol you build the file "
                            "with.")
    row = s.field(row, "Currency code", cfg.currency_code,
                  validate=list_from("CurrencyList"))
    row = s.field(row, "Today", None, style="date", name="TodayDate",
                  mode="calc", formula="=TODAY()",
                  help_text="Live. Everything that says 'overdue', "
                            "'due soon' or 'this week' reads this "
                            "one cell.")
    row = s.field(row, "Reporting year", cfg.report_year, style="int",
                  name="ReportYear", validate=number_check(2000, 2100),
                  help_text="The year the Profit Analysis, Monthly "
                            "and Annual reports cover.")
    row = s.field(row, "Year starts", None, style="date",
                  name="YearStart", mode="calc",
                  formula="=DATE(ReportYear,1,1)")
    row = s.field(row, "Year ends", None, style="date",
                  name="YearEnd", mode="calc",
                  formula="=DATE(ReportYear,12,31)")
    row = s.field(row, "This month", None, style="text",
                  name="ThisMonthKey", mode="calc",
                  formula='=TEXT(TODAY(),"yyyy-mm")',
                  help_text="The month key every monthly total "
                            "groups on.")
    row += 1

    # ------------------------------------------------- labour rates --
    row = s.section(row, "What labour costs you", LAST_COL)
    row = s.note(
        row,
        "The crew cost rate is the most important number in this "
        "workbook and the one most often set too low. It is the "
        "loaded cost of an hour of somebody's time - wages, payroll "
        "tax, insurance, holiday, the share of the truck they ride "
        "in - not the figure on their payslip. A $20 wage usually "
        "costs a business between $26 and $30.",
        LAST_COL, height=46)
    row += 1
    row = s.field(row, "Crew cost per hour", cfg.labour_rate,
                  style="money", name="LabourRate",
                  validate=number_check(0, 1000),
                  help_text="Used wherever a person on the Crew "
                            "Database has no rate of their own.")
    row = s.field(row, "Charge-out rate per hour", cfg.charge_rate,
                  style="money", name="ChargeRate",
                  validate=number_check(0, 1000),
                  help_text="What an hour of crew time is worth to "
                            "a customer. Every 'earns per hour' "
                            "column in the workbook is measured "
                            "against this.")
    row = s.field(row, "Overhead per crew hour", cfg.overhead_per_hour,
                  style="money", name="OverheadRate",
                  validate=number_check(0, 1000),
                  help_text="The yard, the insurance, the phone and "
                            "the paperwork, spread over the hours "
                            "you actually work. Take your yearly "
                            "overheads and divide by your billable "
                            "hours - most small operations land "
                            "between $8 and $15.")
    row = s.field(row, "Standard working day", cfg.working_hours_day,
                  style="number1", name="WorkingHours",
                  validate=number_check(1, 24),
                  help_text="Hours in a normal day, used by the crew "
                            "schedule when no times are given.")
    row = s.field(row, "Overtime after", cfg.overtime_after_hours,
                  style="number1", name="OvertimeAfter",
                  validate=number_check(0, 168),
                  help_text="Hours per person per week before "
                            "overtime starts. An indicator for your "
                            "own planning - your payroll provider is "
                            "the authority on overtime law.")
    row = s.field(row, "Overtime multiplier", cfg.overtime_multiplier,
                  style="number1", name="OvertimeRate",
                  validate=number_check(1, 5))
    row = s.field(row, "Default crew size", cfg.crew_size, style="int",
                  name="CrewSize", validate=number_check(1, 20),
                  help_text="How many people go out on a normal "
                            "job.")
    row += 1

    # --------------------------------------------------- the pricing --
    row = s.section(row, "How you price work", LAST_COL)
    row = s.note(
        row,
        "The quote calculator works out what a job costs you, then "
        "adds the target margin. The minimum margin is the line "
        "below which the workbook starts calling a price a problem - "
        "on a quote, on a service, on a customer and on a contract.",
        LAST_COL, height=38)
    row += 1
    row = s.field(row, "Target profit margin", cfg.target_margin,
                  style="percent", name="TargetMargin",
                  validate=number_check(0, 0.95),
                  help_text="Margin, not markup. 45% margin is the "
                            "same as a 1.82 multiplier on cost.")
    row = s.field(row, "Minimum acceptable margin", cfg.minimum_margin,
                  style="percent", name="MinMargin",
                  validate=number_check(0, 0.95),
                  help_text="Anything below this is flagged in red "
                            "wherever it appears.")
    row = s.field(row, "Minimum visit charge", cfg.minimum_visit,
                  style="money", name="MinimumVisit",
                  validate=number_check(0, 10000),
                  help_text="The least you will turn out for. The "
                            "quote calculator uses it as a floor.")
    row = s.field(row, "Materials markup", cfg.material_markup,
                  style="number1", name="MaterialMarkup",
                  validate=number_check(1, 10),
                  help_text="Multiplier on what materials cost you. "
                            "1.35 means a $100 pallet of mulch is "
                            "charged at $135 - collecting, carrying "
                            "and spreading are real work.")
    row = s.field(row, "Travel rate per mile", cfg.travel_rate_mile,
                  style="money", name="TravelRate",
                  validate=number_check(0, 100),
                  help_text="Fuel, tyres and the share of the truck. "
                            "Charged on the round trip, both ways.")
    row = s.field(row, "Default drive time", cfg.drive_time_minutes,
                  style="int", name="DriveMinutes",
                  validate=number_check(0, 600),
                  help_text="Minutes each way, used when a property "
                            "has no drive time of its own.")
    row = s.field(row, "Mowing minutes per 1,000 sq ft",
                  cfg.mow_minutes_per_1000, style="number1",
                  name="MowMinutesPer1000",
                  validate=number_check(0, 600),
                  help_text="Your own measured pace with your own "
                            "machine, for one person. Used to "
                            "estimate mow time on the Property "
                            "Database.")
    row = s.field(row, "Quote valid for", cfg.quote_valid_days,
                  style="int", name="QuoteValidDays",
                  validate=number_check(1, 365),
                  help_text="Days before a quote expires on its own.")
    row += 1

    # ------------------------------------------------------- the tax --
    row = s.section(row, "Tax", LAST_COL)
    row = s.note(
        row,
        "Whether lawn care is taxable where you trade is a question "
        "for your accountant, and the answer differs by state and by "
        "country - mowing and landscaping are often treated "
        "differently from each other. Switch tax on only when you "
        "know which applies to you, and set the rate you are "
        "actually registered for.",
        LAST_COL, height=46)
    row += 1
    row = s.field(row, "Charge tax on services?",
                  "Yes" if cfg.tax_on_services else "No",
                  name="TaxOnServices", validate=yes_no(),
                  help_text="No means no tax is added anywhere in "
                            "the workbook.")
    row = s.field(row, "Tax rate", cfg.tax_rate, style="percent",
                  name="TaxRate", validate=number_check(0, 1))
    row = s.field(row, "What you call it", cfg.tax_name,
                  name="TaxName", value_end=5,
                  help_text="Sales tax, VAT, GST - whatever appears "
                            "on your paperwork.")
    row += 1

    # -------------------------------------------------- when to warn --
    row = s.section(row, "When the workbook should warn you", LAST_COL)
    row = s.note(
        row,
        "Every 'due', 'overdue' and 'chase' message in the workbook "
        "is measured against one of these. Make them match how you "
        "actually work - a round that runs a fortnight ahead needs a "
        "different upcoming window from one booked the night before.",
        LAST_COL, height=38)
    row += 1
    row = s.field(row, "Upcoming window", cfg.upcoming_days,
                  style="int", name="UpcomingDays",
                  validate=number_check(1, 90),
                  help_text="Days ahead counted as 'coming up' on "
                            "the dashboard.")
    row = s.field(row, "Invoice overdue after", cfg.overdue_days,
                  style="int", name="OverdueDays",
                  validate=number_check(0, 90),
                  help_text="Days past the due date before an "
                            "invoice is called overdue.")
    row = s.field(row, "Chase a quote after", cfg.chase_quote_days,
                  style="int", name="ChaseQuoteDays",
                  validate=number_check(1, 180),
                  help_text="Days with no answer before the quote "
                            "appears on the chase list.")
    row = s.field(row, "Contract renewal warning",
                  cfg.renewal_warning_days, style="int",
                  name="RenewalWarnDays", validate=number_check(1, 365),
                  help_text="Days before a review or end date that "
                            "the reminder starts.")
    row = s.field(row, "Customer dormant after", cfg.dormant_days,
                  style="int", name="DormantDays",
                  validate=number_check(7, 999),
                  help_text="Days since the last completed visit "
                            "before a customer is called dormant.")
    row = s.field(row, "Chase a review after", cfg.feedback_chase_days,
                  style="int", name="FeedbackChaseDays",
                  validate=number_check(1, 180))
    row += 1

    # --------------------------------------------------- the machines --
    row = s.section(row, "Equipment servicing", LAST_COL)
    row = s.note(
        row,
        "Mowers measure their lives in engine hours and everything "
        "else measures it in months, so the workbook tracks both and "
        "warns on whichever comes first. These are the defaults; any "
        "machine can carry its own interval on the Equipment "
        "Inventory tab.",
        LAST_COL, height=38)
    row += 1
    row = s.field(row, "Service every (hours)", cfg.service_hours,
                  style="int", name="ServiceHours",
                  validate=number_check(1, 10000),
                  help_text="Engine hours between services. Check "
                            "the manual - 50 to 100 hours is "
                            "typical for commercial mowers.")
    row = s.field(row, "Service every (months)", cfg.service_months,
                  style="int", name="ServiceMonths",
                  validate=number_check(1, 120))
    row = s.field(row, "Warn this many hours before",
                  cfg.service_warning_hours, style="int",
                  name="ServiceWarnHours", validate=number_check(0, 500))
    row = s.field(row, "Warn this many days before",
                  cfg.service_warning_days, style="int",
                  name="ServiceWarnDays", validate=number_check(0, 365))
    row += 1

    # ---------------------------------------------------- the season --
    row = s.section(row, "Your season and your targets", LAST_COL)
    row = s.field(row, "Season starts (month)", cfg.season_start_month,
                  style="int", name="SeasonStart",
                  validate=number_check(1, 12),
                  help_text="The first month of your main cutting "
                            "season. 3 is March.")
    row = s.field(row, "Season ends (month)", cfg.season_end_month,
                  style="int", name="SeasonEnd",
                  validate=number_check(1, 12),
                  help_text="The last month. 11 is November.")
    row = s.field(row, "Revenue target for the year",
                  cfg.target_revenue_year, style="money",
                  name="RevenueTarget",
                  validate=number_check(0, 100000000),
                  help_text="What you want the year to bring in. The "
                            "monthly target is a twelfth of this "
                            "unless you set your own.")
    row = s.field(row, "Jobs per week target", cfg.target_jobs_week,
                  style="int", name="JobsTarget",
                  validate=number_check(0, 1000))
    row = s.field(row, "Fuel price per gallon", cfg.fuel_price,
                  style="money", name="FuelPrice",
                  validate=number_check(0, 100),
                  help_text="For estimating. Actual spend comes off "
                            "the Vehicle & Fuel Tracker.")
    row += 2

    # ------------------------------------------- payment terms table --
    row = s.section(row, "Payment terms", LAST_COL)
    row = s.note(
        row,
        "What each term means in days. Every invoice works out its "
        "own due date from this table, so if your 'Net 30' really "
        "means thirty days from the end of the month, change the "
        "number here. 'Monthly in advance' is negative because the "
        "money is due before the work.",
        LAST_COL, height=38)
    row += 1
    terms_head = row
    s.table_head(terms_head, 1, ["Term", "Days"], widths=[26, 14])
    first_term = terms_head + 1
    for index, (label, days) in enumerate(PAYMENT_TERMS):
        line = first_term + index
        s.write(line, 1, label, fmts.input("text"))
        s.write(line, 2, days, fmts.input("int"))
    last_term = first_term + len(PAYMENT_TERMS) - 1
    book.define_name(
        "PaymentTermsList",
        f"={sheet_ref}!$B${first_term + 1}:$B${last_term + 1}")
    book.define_name(
        "TermDays",
        f"={sheet_ref}!$C${first_term + 1}:$C${last_term + 1}")
    row = last_term + 2

    # ----------------------------------------------- frequency table --
    row = s.section(row, "How often work repeats", LAST_COL)
    row = s.note(
        row,
        "This table turns 'Fortnightly' into a number of visits and "
        "a number of days. The contracts tab uses the days to work "
        "out when the next visit is due, and the annual value of "
        "every agreement is the price per visit times the visits a "
        "year. 'One-off' is zero on both counts.",
        LAST_COL, height=38)
    row += 1
    freq_head = row
    s.table_head(freq_head, 1,
                 ["How often", "Visits a year", "Days between"],
                 widths=[24, 16, 16])
    first_freq = freq_head + 1
    for index, (label, per_year, gap) in enumerate(FREQUENCIES):
        line = first_freq + index
        s.write(line, 1, label, fmts.input("text"))
        s.write(line, 2, per_year, fmts.input("int"))
        s.write(line, 3, gap, fmts.input("int"))
    last_freq = first_freq + len(FREQUENCIES) - 1
    book.define_name(
        "FrequencyList",
        f"={sheet_ref}!$B${first_freq + 1}:$B${last_freq + 1}")
    book.define_name(
        "VisitsPerYear",
        f"={sheet_ref}!$C${first_freq + 1}:$C${last_freq + 1}")
    book.define_name(
        "DaysBetween",
        f"={sheet_ref}!$D${first_freq + 1}:$D${last_freq + 1}")
    row = last_freq + 2

    # -------------------------------------------------------- prefixes --
    row = s.section(row, "ID prefixes", LAST_COL)
    row = s.note(
        row,
        "The letters in front of every automatic ID. These are the "
        "keys the workbook joins on - Customer IDs, Property IDs, "
        "Job IDs and Invoice IDs above all. Change the prefixes here "
        "if you want to, but do it before you have typed two hundred "
        "customers, not after: existing rows keep the ID they were "
        "given.",
        LAST_COL, height=44)
    row += 1
    half = (len(ID_PREFIX_FIELDS) + 1) // 2
    top = row
    for index, (label, default, name, _digits, hint) in enumerate(
            ID_PREFIX_FIELDS):
        column = 0 if index < half else 4
        line = top + (index if index < half else index - half)
        s.write(line, column + 1, label, fmts.chrome("label_bold"))
        s.write(line, column + 2, default, fmts.input("text"))
        s.write(line, column + 3, hint, fmts.chrome("note"))
        book.define_name(
            name, f"={sheet_ref}!${col_letter(column + 2)}${line + 1}")
    row = top + half + 1

    # ----------------------------------------------------------- lists --
    row = s.section(row, "Drop-down lists", LAST_COL)
    row = s.note(
        row,
        "Add new entries directly under the last one in a column - "
        "every list has room to grow and blank rows are ignored. Do "
        "not delete a whole column: a drop-down pointing at a "
        "missing list stops working. The crew names and vehicle "
        "names are yours to rename - 'Crew A' and 'Truck 1' are "
        "only a starting point.",
        LAST_COL, height=44)
    row += 1

    per_row = 4
    spare = 6
    top = row
    column = 0
    tallest = 0
    for index, (name, values) in enumerate(LISTS):
        if index and index % per_row == 0:
            top += tallest + 2
            column = 0
            tallest = 0
        s.write(top, column, name.replace("List", ""),
                fmts.chrome("header"))
        s.set_col(column, column, 28)
        for offset, value in enumerate(values):
            s.write(top + 1 + offset, column, value, fmts.input("text"))
        for offset in range(len(values), len(values) + spare):
            s.write(top + 1 + offset, column, None, fmts.input("text"))
        first = top + 2
        last = top + 1 + len(values) + spare
        book.define_name(
            name,
            f"={sheet_ref}!${col_letter(column)}${first}:"
            f"${col_letter(column)}${last}")
        tallest = max(tallest, len(values) + spare)
        column += 3
    row = top + tallest + 3

    # ------------------------------------------------------ month keys --
    row = s.section(row, "Month keys", LAST_COL)
    row = s.note(
        row,
        "The months the reports pick from, in plain yyyy-mm text so "
        "that every monthly total in the workbook groups on exactly "
        "the same thing.",
        LAST_COL, height=28)
    row += 1
    keys = _month_keys(cfg.today)
    s.write(row, 0, "Month", fmts.chrome("header"))
    s.set_col(0, 0, 14)
    for offset, key in enumerate(keys):
        s.write(row + 1 + offset, 0, key, fmts.input("text"))
    book.define_name(
        "MonthKeyList",
        f"={sheet_ref}!$A${row + 2}:$A${row + 1 + len(keys)}")
    book.define_name(
        "MonthKeys",
        dynamic_name(NAME, "A", row + 2, row + 1 + len(keys)))
    row = row + len(keys) + 2

    row = s.note(row, ROUTE_NOTE, LAST_COL, height=62)
    row += 1
    row = s.note(row, PAYROLL_NOTE, LAST_COL, height=56)
    row += 1
    row = s.note(row, WEATHER_NOTE, LAST_COL, height=48)
    row += 1
    row = s.note(row, PHOTO_NOTE, LAST_COL, height=48)
    row += 1
    row = s.note(row, HONESTY_NOTE, LAST_COL, height=68)
    row += 1
    row = s.note(row, PRIVACY_NOTE, LAST_COL, height=56)
    row += 1
    row = s.note(row, DISCLAIMER, LAST_COL, height=76)

    s.finish(freeze_row=4, gridlines=False, fit_width=True)
    s.protect()
    return s
````

### `landscaping_spreadsheet/sheets/dashboard.py`

*383 lines · 4 functions*

> `Dashboard` - the page that answers "what do I need to do today?"
>
> Everything here is worked out from the other tabs, so nothing is ever
> out of date and there is nothing to refresh. The questions are in the
> order somebody running a lawn care round actually asks them: what is
> on today, what has slipped, who owes me money, what is about to break,
> and is any of this actually making a profit.
>
> The top strip is deliberately the week rather than the year. A
> landscaping business is run a week at a time; the year is something
> you look at in January.

| Defines | What it does |
|---------|--------------|
| `_alert_count(sheet, prefix)` | How many rows on a register are shouting a given prefix |
| `_all_alerts(prefix)` | Total rows across the workbook whose alert starts with prefix |
| `_by_rank(sheet, rank_key, field, n)` | The nth row of a register by one of its hidden rank columns |
| `build(book, fmts, cfg, reg)` |  |

**Module constants:** `NAME` ('Dashboard'), `LAST_COL` (12), `LIST_ROWS` (8), `ALERT_SHEETS` (16 items).

````python
"""`Dashboard` - the page that answers "what do I need to do today?"

Everything here is worked out from the other tabs, so nothing is ever
out of date and there is nothing to refresh. The questions are in the
order somebody running a lawn care round actually asks them: what is
on today, what has slipped, who owes me money, what is about to break,
and is any of this actually making a profit.

The top strip is deliberately the week rather than the year. A
landscaping business is run a week at a time; the year is something
you look at in January.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from ..config import Config, Palette
from ..layout import Registry
from ..writer import SheetWriter

NAME = "Dashboard"
LAST_COL = 12
LIST_ROWS = 8


def _alert_count(sheet: str, prefix: str) -> str:
    """How many rows on a register are shouting a given prefix."""
    return f'COUNTIF([[{sheet}.alert]],"{prefix}*")'


#: every register that carries a "What needs doing" column
ALERT_SHEETS = [
    "Customer CRM", "Property Database", "Service Catalog",
    "Quote Calculator", "Job & Work Orders", "Recurring Contracts",
    "Crew Database", "Crew Schedule", "Timesheets & Payroll",
    "Equipment Inventory", "Vehicle & Fuel Tracker",
    "Materials Inventory", "Invoice & Payments", "Expense Tracker",
    "Customer Feedback", "Profit Analysis",
]


def _all_alerts(prefix: str) -> str:
    """Total rows across the workbook whose alert starts with prefix."""
    return "+".join(_alert_count(sheet, prefix)
                    for sheet in ALERT_SHEETS)


def _by_rank(sheet: str, rank_key: str, field: str, n: int) -> str:
    """The nth row of a register by one of its hidden rank columns."""
    return (f'IFERROR(INDEX([[{sheet}.{field}]],MATCH({n},'
            f'[[{sheet}.{rank_key}]],0)),"")')


def build(book, fmts, cfg: Config, reg: Registry) -> SheetWriter:
    s = SheetWriter(book, fmts, cfg, reg, NAME, tab_color=Palette.deep)

    s.banner("Landscaping Business Dashboard",
             "Today, this week, the money and the machines - worked "
             "out from the tabs you have already filled in.",
             LAST_COL)
    s.set_col(0, 0, 26)
    for column in range(1, LAST_COL + 1):
        s.set_col(column, column, 13)

    # ------------------------------------------------------------ head --
    row = 4
    s.merge(row, 0, 0, "Business", fmts.chrome("label_bold"))
    s.merge(row, 1, 3, None, fmts.calc("text"))
    s.formula(row, 1, "=BusinessName", fmts.calc("text"))
    s.merge(row, 4, 4, "Today", fmts.chrome("label_right"))
    s.formula(row, 5, "=TodayDate", fmts.calc("date"))
    s.merge(row, 6, 6, "Customers", fmts.chrome("label_right"))
    s.formula(row, 7, "=SUM(CustIsActive)", fmts.calc("int"))
    s.merge(row, 8, 8, "Properties", fmts.chrome("label_right"))
    s.formula(row, 9, "=SUM(PropIsActive)", fmts.calc("int"))
    s.merge(row, 10, LAST_COL, None, fmts.chrome("note"))
    s.formula(
        row, 10,
        '="Figures update the moment you type. Nothing to refresh."',
        fmts.chrome("note"))
    row += 2

    # -------------------------------------------------------- the week --
    row = s.section(row, "Today and the week ahead", LAST_COL)
    s.kpi(row, 0, "Jobs today", "=SUM(JobIsToday)", "int",
          note="Booked for today and not yet closed off")
    s.kpi(row, 2, "Jobs this week", "=SUM(JobIsWeek)", "int",
          note="Within the upcoming window on Settings")
    s.kpi(row, 4, "Hours booked",
          '=SUMIFS(JobHoursEst,JobIsOpen,1,JobDateNum,'
          '">="&TodayDate,JobDateNum,"<="&TodayDate+UpcomingDays)',
          "number1", note="Crew hours the week needs")
    s.kpi(row, 6, "Worth this week",
          '=SUMIFS(JobCharge,JobIsOpen,1,JobDateNum,">="&TodayDate,'
          'JobDateNum,"<="&TodayDate+UpcomingDays)', "money",
          note="Value of what is booked")
    s.kpi(row, 8, "Unassigned",
          '=COUNTIFS(JobIsOpen,1,JobCrew,"Unassigned")', "int",
          note="Booked with nobody to do it")
    row = s.kpi(row, 10, "Behind schedule",
                '=COUNTIFS(JobIsOpen,1,JobDateNum,"<"&TodayDate)',
                "int", span=3,
                note="Past their date and still open")
    row += 1

    # ------------------------------------------------- work that slipped --
    row = s.section(row, "Work you still owe", LAST_COL)
    row = s.note(
        row,
        "Rain, breakdowns and locked gates are part of the trade. "
        "What costs money is forgetting about them. Everything in "
        "this row is work that was promised and has not happened.",
        LAST_COL, height=30)
    row += 1
    s.kpi(row, 0, "Rained off / revisit", "=SUM(JobIsCatchup)", "int",
          note="Waiting to be rescheduled")
    s.kpi(row, 2, "Contract visits due", "=SUM(ConIsDue)", "int",
          note="Due by the contract and not booked")
    s.kpi(row, 4, "Quotes waiting",
          f"={_alert_count('Quote Calculator', 'Chase -')}", "int",
          note="Sent, no answer, past the chase window")
    s.kpi(row, 6, "Quotes to convert",
          f"={_alert_count('Quote Calculator', 'Action -')}", "int",
          note="Accepted with no work order raised")
    s.kpi(row, 8, "Completed, unbilled", "=SUM(JobIsUnbilled)", "int",
          note="Done and never invoiced")
    row = s.kpi(row, 10, "Value unbilled",
                "=SUMPRODUCT(JobIsUnbilled*JobCharge)", "money",
                span=3, note="Money sitting on the job sheet")
    row += 1

    # ------------------------------------------------------- the money --
    row = s.section(row, "The money", LAST_COL)
    s.kpi(row, 0, "Owed to you", "=SUM(InvOutstanding)", "money",
          note="Invoiced and not yet paid")
    s.kpi(row, 2, "Overdue", "=SUMPRODUCT(InvIsOverdue*InvOutstanding)",
          "money", note="Past the due date - chase these")
    s.kpi(row, 4, "Over 60 days",
          '=SUMIFS(InvOutstanding,InvDaysLate,">=60")', "money",
          note="The ones that need a decision")
    s.kpi(row, 6, "Revenue this month",
          "=SUMIFS(JobCharge,JobMonth,ThisMonthKey,JobIsDone,1)",
          "money", note="Completed visits, excluding tax")
    s.kpi(row, 8, "Costs this month",
          '=SUMIFS(ExpAmount,ExpMonth,ThisMonthKey)+'
          'SUMIFS(TimeCost,TimeMonth,ThisMonthKey)+'
          'SUMIFS(VehAmount,VehMonth,ThisMonthKey)', "money",
          note="Labour, expenses and vehicles")
    row = s.kpi(row, 10, "Booked recurring",
                "=SUMPRODUCT(ConIsLive*ConAnnualValue)", "money",
                span=3, note="Annual value of live contracts")
    row += 1

    # ---------------------------------------------------- the machines --
    row = s.section(row, "Machines, materials and people", LAST_COL)
    s.kpi(row, 0, "Service overdue", "=SUM(EquipServiceDue)", "int",
          note="By hours or by date, whichever came first")
    s.kpi(row, 2, "Service soon", "=SUM(EquipServiceSoon)", "int",
          note="Inside the warning window")
    s.kpi(row, 4, "Out of service",
          '=COUNTIF(EquipStatus,"Out of service")', "int",
          note="Cannot be used at all")
    s.kpi(row, 6, "Materials low", "=SUM(MatIsLow)", "int",
          note="At or below the reorder point")
    s.kpi(row, 8, "Cost to restock", "=SUM(MatOrderValue)", "money",
          note="What the shopping list comes to")
    row = s.kpi(row, 10, "Licences expiring",
                f"={_alert_count('Crew Database', 'Renew -')}+"
                f"{_alert_count('Crew Database', 'Expired -')}",
                "int", span=3, note="Crew tickets about to lapse")
    row += 1

    # ------------------------------------------------- is it working? --
    row = s.section(row, "Is the work paying?", LAST_COL)
    row = s.note(
        row,
        "Revenue is not the test. Profit per crew hour is: it is "
        "what is left after wages, materials, fuel and the "
        "overheads, divided by the hours it took. If it falls while "
        "revenue rises, the extra work is costing you money.",
        LAST_COL, height=34)
    row += 1
    s.kpi(row, 0, "Profit this month",
          '=IFERROR(SUMIFS(ProfitProfit,ProfitMonth,ThisMonthKey),0)',
          "money", note="From the Profit Analysis tab")
    s.kpi(row, 2, "Margin",
          '=IFERROR(SUMIFS(ProfitProfit,ProfitMonth,ThisMonthKey)/'
          'SUMIFS(ProfitRevenue,ProfitMonth,ThisMonthKey),"")',
          "percent0", note="Against your target on Settings")
    s.kpi(row, 4, "Profit per crew hour",
          '=IFERROR(SUMIFS(ProfitProfitHour,ProfitMonth,'
          'ThisMonthKey),"")', "money",
          note="The number that matters most")
    s.kpi(row, 6, "Billable hours",
          '=IFERROR(SUMIFS(ProfitBillablePct,ProfitMonth,'
          'ThisMonthKey),"")', "percent0",
          note="Share of paid time that was sold")
    s.kpi(row, 8, "Quote win rate",
          '=IFERROR(SUM(QuoteIsWon)/(SUM(QuoteIsWon)+'
          'COUNTIF(QuoteStatus,"Declined")),"")', "percent0",
          note="Accepted against answered")
    row = s.kpi(row, 10, "Average visit",
                '=IFERROR(SUMIFS(JobCharge,JobIsDone,1)/'
                'SUM(JobIsDone),"")', "money", span=3,
                note="Across every completed visit")
    row += 1

    # ------------------------------------------------- what needs doing --
    row = s.section(row, "What needs doing", LAST_COL)
    row = s.note(
        row,
        "Every register has a 'What needs doing' column. This is all "
        "sixteen of them added up, by how loudly they are shouting. "
        "Work down from the left.",
        LAST_COL, height=30)
    row += 1
    s.kpi(row, 0, "Urgent", f"={_all_alerts('Urgent -')}", "int",
          note="Something is wrong, not just late")
    s.kpi(row, 2, "Overdue", f"={_all_alerts('Overdue -')}", "int",
          note="Past a date that mattered")
    s.kpi(row, 4, "Chase",
          f"={_all_alerts('Chase -')}+{_all_alerts('Send -')}+"
          f"{_all_alerts('Follow up -')}", "int",
          note="Somebody is waiting on you")
    s.kpi(row, 6, "Schedule",
          f"={_all_alerts('Schedule -')}+"
          f"{_all_alerts('Reschedule -')}", "int",
          note="Work to get in the diary")
    s.kpi(row, 8, "Service & reorder",
          f"={_all_alerts('Service -')}+{_all_alerts('Reorder -')}+"
          f"{_all_alerts('Order -')}+{_all_alerts('Renew -')}", "int",
          note="Machines, materials, contracts")
    row = s.kpi(row, 10, "Tidy up",
                f"={_all_alerts('Add -')}+{_all_alerts('Check -')}+"
                f"{_all_alerts('Action -')}+"
                f"{_all_alerts('Missing -')}", "int", span=3,
                note="Gaps in the record")
    row += 2

    # ------------------------------------------------ today's work list --
    row = s.section(row, "Today's jobs", LAST_COL)
    s.table_head(row, 0,
                 ["Job ID", "Crew", "Start", "Customer", "Address",
                  "Service", "Charge"],
                 widths=[12, 12, 9, 20, 28, 22, 11])
    head = row
    for n in range(1, LIST_ROWS + 1):
        line = head + n
        pick = ('IFERROR(INDEX([[Job & Work Orders.{f}]],'
                'MATCH(ROW()-{h},[[Job & Work Orders.today_rank]],0)),'
                '"")')
        s.formula(line, 0,
                  "=" + pick.format(f="id", h=head + 1),
                  fmts.calc("id"))
        s.formula(line, 1, "=" + pick.format(f="crew", h=head + 1),
                  fmts.calc("text"))
        s.formula(line, 2,
                  '=IFERROR(IF(INDEX([[Job & Work Orders.start_time]],'
                  f'MATCH(ROW()-{head + 1},'
                  '[[Job & Work Orders.today_rank]],0))="","",'
                  'INDEX([[Job & Work Orders.start_time]],'
                  f'MATCH(ROW()-{head + 1},'
                  '[[Job & Work Orders.today_rank]],0))),"")',
                  fmts.calc("time"))
        s.formula(line, 3,
                  "=" + pick.format(f="customer_name", h=head + 1),
                  fmts.calc("text"))
        s.formula(line, 4, "=" + pick.format(f="address", h=head + 1),
                  fmts.calc("text"))
        s.formula(line, 5, "=" + pick.format(f="service", h=head + 1),
                  fmts.calc("text"))
        s.formula(line, 6,
                  '=IFERROR(INDEX([[Job & Work Orders.charge]],'
                  f'MATCH(ROW()-{head + 1},'
                  '[[Job & Work Orders.today_rank]],0))*1,"")',
                  fmts.calc("money"))
    row = head + LIST_ROWS + 2
    row = s.note(
        row,
        "The first eight jobs booked for today, in the order the "
        "Route Planner would print them. Open that tab for the full "
        "day and a sheet the crew can take with them.",
        LAST_COL, height=28)
    row += 1

    # ---------------------------------------------------- chase list --
    row = s.section(row, "Chase these first", LAST_COL)
    s.table_head(row, 0,
                 ["Invoice", "Customer", "Due", "Days late",
                  "Outstanding", "Reminders"],
                 widths=[12, 24, 12, 11, 13, 11])
    head = row
    for n in range(1, LIST_ROWS + 1):
        line = head + n
        s.formula(line, 0,
                  "=" + _by_rank("Invoice & Payments", "late_rank",
                                 "id", f"ROW()-{head + 1}"),
                  fmts.calc("id"))
        s.formula(line, 1,
                  "=" + _by_rank("Invoice & Payments", "late_rank",
                                 "customer_name", f"ROW()-{head + 1}"),
                  fmts.calc("text"))
        s.formula(line, 2,
                  '=IFERROR(IF(INDEX([[Invoice & Payments.due_date]],'
                  f'MATCH(ROW()-{head + 1},'
                  '[[Invoice & Payments.late_rank]],0))="","",'
                  'INDEX([[Invoice & Payments.due_date]],'
                  f'MATCH(ROW()-{head + 1},'
                  '[[Invoice & Payments.late_rank]],0))),"")',
                  fmts.calc("date"))
        s.formula(line, 3,
                  '=IFERROR(INDEX([[Invoice & Payments.days_late]],'
                  f'MATCH(ROW()-{head + 1},'
                  '[[Invoice & Payments.late_rank]],0))*1,"")',
                  fmts.calc("int"))
        s.formula(line, 4,
                  '=IFERROR(INDEX([[Invoice & Payments.outstanding]],'
                  f'MATCH(ROW()-{head + 1},'
                  '[[Invoice & Payments.late_rank]],0))*1,"")',
                  fmts.calc("money"))
        s.formula(line, 5,
                  '=IFERROR(INDEX([[Invoice & Payments.reminders]],'
                  f'MATCH(ROW()-{head + 1},'
                  '[[Invoice & Payments.late_rank]],0))*1,"")',
                  fmts.calc("int"))
    s.cf(f"D{head + 2}:D{head + 1 + LIST_ROWS}",
         {"type": "cell", "criteria": ">=", "value": 30,
          "format": fmts.cf("bad")})
    row = head + LIST_ROWS + 2

    # --------------------------------------------------- best customers --
    row = s.section(row, "Your best customers", LAST_COL)
    s.table_head(row, 0,
                 ["Customer", "Visits", "Lifetime revenue", "Profit",
                  "Margin", "Contract / yr", "Last visit"],
                 widths=[26, 10, 16, 12, 10, 13, 12])
    head = row
    for n in range(1, LIST_ROWS + 1):
        line = head + n
        rank = f"ROW()-{head + 1}"
        s.formula(line, 0,
                  "=" + _by_rank("Customer CRM", "lifetime_rank",
                                 "name", rank), fmts.calc("text"))
        s.formula(line, 1,
                  '=IFERROR(INDEX([[Customer CRM.jobs_done]],'
                  f'MATCH({rank},[[Customer CRM.lifetime_rank]],0))*1,'
                  '"")', fmts.calc("int"))
        s.formula(line, 2,
                  '=IFERROR(INDEX([[Customer CRM.lifetime]],'
                  f'MATCH({rank},[[Customer CRM.lifetime_rank]],0))*1,'
                  '"")', fmts.calc("money"))
        s.formula(line, 3,
                  '=IFERROR(INDEX([[Customer CRM.profit]],'
                  f'MATCH({rank},[[Customer CRM.lifetime_rank]],0))*1,'
                  '"")', fmts.calc("money"))
        s.formula(line, 4,
                  '=IFERROR(INDEX([[Customer CRM.margin_pct]],'
                  f'MATCH({rank},[[Customer CRM.lifetime_rank]],0))*1,'
                  '"")', fmts.calc("percent"))
        s.formula(line, 5,
                  '=IFERROR(INDEX([[Customer CRM.contract_value]],'
                  f'MATCH({rank},[[Customer CRM.lifetime_rank]],0))*1,'
                  '"")', fmts.calc("money"))
        s.formula(line, 6,
                  '=IFERROR(IF(INDEX([[Customer CRM.last_job]],'
                  f'MATCH({rank},[[Customer CRM.lifetime_rank]],0))="",'
                  '"",INDEX([[Customer CRM.last_job]],'
                  f'MATCH({rank},[[Customer CRM.lifetime_rank]],0))),'
                  '"")', fmts.calc("date"))
    row = head + LIST_ROWS + 2

    # ------------------------------------------------------ the honest bit --
    row = s.note(
        row,
        "What this dashboard is not: it does not know about work "
        "nobody has written down. Every figure above is built from "
        "the job, timesheet, expense and invoice rows you have "
        "entered. A week of visits recorded on the back of an "
        "envelope is a week this page cannot see.",
        LAST_COL, height=44)

    s.finish(freeze_row=4, gridlines=False, fit_width=True)
    s.protect()
    return s
````

### `landscaping_spreadsheet/sheets/getting_started.py`

*240 lines · 1 function*

> `Getting Started` - the hour that makes the other twenty-two tabs work.
>
> Written in the order somebody actually does it, with the honest bits
> included: what this workbook does, what it does not do, and the four
> IDs that hold the whole thing together.

| Defines | What it does |
|---------|--------------|
| `build(book, fmts, cfg, reg)` |  |

**Module constants:** `NAME` ('Getting Started'), `LAST_COL` (9), `STEPS` (10 items), `WHAT_IT_DOES` (9 items), `WHAT_IT_DOES_NOT` (6 items).

````python
"""`Getting Started` - the hour that makes the other twenty-two tabs work.

Written in the order somebody actually does it, with the honest bits
included: what this workbook does, what it does not do, and the four
IDs that hold the whole thing together.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from ..config import (DISCLAIMER, HONESTY_NOTE, PAYROLL_NOTE,
                      PHOTO_NOTE, PRIVACY_NOTE, ROUTE_NOTE,
                      WEATHER_NOTE, Config, Palette)
from ..layout import Registry
from ..writer import SheetWriter

NAME = "Getting Started"
LAST_COL = 9

STEPS = [
    ("1. Settings & Lists - half an hour, once",
     "Your business details, your crew cost per hour, your "
     "charge-out rate, your overhead per hour, your margins and "
     "your warning windows. Everything else in the workbook is "
     "calculated from these. The crew cost rate and the overhead "
     "rate are the two that decide whether your profit figures are "
     "true - set them properly before anything else."),
    ("2. Service Catalog - your price list",
     "Thirty services come filled in with rates, units and times. "
     "They are plausible starting points for a two-person crew, not "
     "a recommendation. Change every rate to yours, and change the "
     "minutes to what the work actually takes you. The margin "
     "column will tell you straight away which of your prices do "
     "not work."),
    ("3. Customer CRM - who pays you",
     "One row per customer, and the Customer ID appears on its own. "
     "A household with two addresses is one customer with two "
     "properties, not two customers."),
    ("4. Property Database - where the work happens",
     "One row per address, pointing at a customer. Measure the lawn "
     "once and measure it properly - the area drives every price, "
     "every time estimate and every material quantity in the "
     "workbook. Record the access, the slope, the obstacles and the "
     "dog while you are there."),
    ("5. Crew Database - who does the work",
     "Each person, with their loaded cost per hour: wages plus "
     "payroll tax plus insurance, not take-home pay. Put the "
     "take-home figure in and every job will look more profitable "
     "than it really is."),
    ("6. Quote Calculator - price the first job",
     "Pick a customer, a property and a service and watch the price "
     "build itself from the lawn size, the labour, the materials, "
     "the travel and the margin you asked for. Override anything "
     "you like; the margin column tells you immediately what the "
     "override cost you."),
    ("7. Job & Work Orders - book the work",
     "A row per visit. Mark it Completed when it is done, and put "
     "the real hours on it. That one habit is what turns this from "
     "a list into a costing system."),
    ("8. Recurring Contracts - the income you can count on",
     "Set up the standing agreements. The workbook tells you when "
     "the next visit is due and what each agreement is worth over a "
     "year - and it will remind you to renew before the season "
     "starts."),
    ("9. Invoice & Payments - get paid",
     "Raise the invoice, put the Job ID on it, record payments as "
     "they arrive. The overdue column is the one that pays for this "
     "workbook."),
    ("10. Timesheets, Expenses, Equipment - the truth",
     "Hours against job numbers, receipts against job numbers, "
     "engine hours against machines. This is the unglamorous part, "
     "and it is the part that makes the Profit Analysis tab worth "
     "reading."),
]

WHAT_IT_DOES = [
    "Prices a job from lawn size, labour hours, materials, "
    "equipment, travel and the profit margin you want.",
    "Keeps customers and properties as separate, linked records, so "
    "one customer can own six addresses.",
    "Works out when every recurring visit is next due, from the "
    "frequency and the last completed visit.",
    "Costs every job twice - what you estimated and what it "
    "actually took - and shows you the gap.",
    "Tracks crew hours, overtime and the share of the day that was "
    "actually billable.",
    "Warns you when a machine is due a service, by engine hours or "
    "by date, whichever comes first.",
    "Chases your overdue invoices, aged into buckets, worst first.",
    "Prints a quote, an invoice and a daily crew work order.",
    "Shows profit by job, by customer, by property, by service and "
    "by month.",
]

WHAT_IT_DOES_NOT = [
    "It does not navigate. The Route Planner organises the stops "
    "you choose onto a printable sheet; it is not GPS and it does "
    "not work out the shortest route.",
    "It does not sync with QuickBooks, Xero, Jobber, Yardbook or "
    "any other software. Nothing in it connects to the internet.",
    "It does not take card payments or send invoices. It records "
    "what you billed and what arrived.",
    "It does not run payroll or calculate tax withholding. It "
    "records hours and costs so your job figures are honest.",
    "It does not book jobs by itself. The contract scheduler tells "
    "you what is due; you decide what actually gets booked, because "
    "weather and breakdowns get a vote.",
    "It does not know about work nobody wrote down. Every figure in "
    "here is built from the rows you enter.",
]


def build(book, fmts, cfg: Config, reg: Registry) -> SheetWriter:
    s = SheetWriter(book, fmts, cfg, reg, NAME,
                    tab_color=Palette.primary_light)

    s.banner("Getting Started",
             "An hour here and the other twenty-two tabs do the rest "
             "of the year for you.",
             LAST_COL)
    s.set_col(0, 0, 4)
    s.set_col(1, 1, 40)
    s.set_col(2, LAST_COL, 14)

    row = 4
    row = s.note(
        row,
        "Work down this list in order. Each step depends on the one "
        "before it - there is no point pricing a job before the "
        "property it happens at exists, and no point costing it "
        "before the crew rate is right. The whole thing takes an "
        "hour or two, once.",
        LAST_COL, height=38)
    row += 1

    # ---------------------------------------------------- the four IDs --
    row = s.section(row, "The four IDs that hold this together",
                    LAST_COL)
    row = s.note(
        row,
        "This is a connected workbook, not twenty-three separate "
        "lists, and four automatic IDs are what connect it. Every "
        "one of them is generated for you, and every cell that "
        "refers to one is a drop-down rather than free text. Pick "
        "from the list - a typed ID is a row that quietly stops "
        "being part of your business.",
        LAST_COL, height=44)
    row += 1
    for label, body in [
        ("Customer ID",
         "Created on the Customer CRM tab. Owns properties, "
         "contracts and invoices. Carries lifetime revenue and "
         "everything they owe you."),
        ("Property ID",
         "Created on the Property Database tab, and points at a "
         "Customer ID. Carries the lawn size, the access, the gate "
         "code and the distance - which is why a quote for an "
         "address you have visited before takes four seconds."),
        ("Job ID",
         "Created on the Job & Work Orders tab, and points at a "
         "property. Timesheets and expenses point at it, which is "
         "how the workbook knows what each visit really cost."),
        ("Invoice ID",
         "Created on the Invoice & Payments tab. Points at a "
         "customer and usually at a job. Carries what is owed and "
         "how late it is."),
    ]:
        s.write(row, 1, label, fmts.chrome("label_bold"))
        s.merge(row, 2, LAST_COL, body, fmts.chrome("label"))
        s.row_height(row, 30)
        row += 1
    row += 1

    # ------------------------------------------------------ the steps --
    row = s.section(row, "Set it up, in this order", LAST_COL)
    for title, body in STEPS:
        s.merge(row, 1, LAST_COL, title, fmts.chrome("subsection"))
        row += 1
        s.merge(row, 1, LAST_COL, body, fmts.chrome("label"))
        s.row_height(row, 42)
        row += 1
    row += 1

    # ------------------------------------------------- the daily habit --
    row = s.section(row, "Then, the daily habit", LAST_COL)
    row = s.bullets(row, [
        "Morning: open the Route Planner, pick the date and the "
        "crew, print one sheet per truck.",
        "Evening: mark the jobs Completed, put the real hours on "
        "them, and type in the day's receipts.",
        "Friday: raise the invoices for the week's completed work. "
        "The dashboard's 'Completed, unbilled' tile is your list.",
        "Monthly: pick the month on the Profit Analysis tab and "
        "read one row. Fifteen minutes, and you will know more "
        "about your business than most people in this trade know "
        "about theirs.",
    ], LAST_COL, height=24)
    row += 1

    # ------------------------------------------------------ honesty --
    row = s.section(row, "What this workbook does", LAST_COL)
    row = s.bullets(row, WHAT_IT_DOES, LAST_COL, height=24)
    row += 1
    row = s.section(row, "What it does not do", LAST_COL)
    row = s.note(
        row,
        "Said plainly, so you can decide before you rely on it.",
        LAST_COL, height=20)
    row += 1
    row = s.bullets(row, WHAT_IT_DOES_NOT, LAST_COL, height=30)
    row += 1

    # -------------------------------------------------- practical bits --
    row = s.section(row, "Practical things worth knowing", LAST_COL)
    row = s.bullets(row, [
        "Every sheet is protected with the password "
        f"'{cfg.password}' so that formulas cannot be typed over by "
        "accident. White cells are yours; shaded ones calculate "
        "themselves.",
        "Add rows by typing in the next empty row - the formulas are "
        "already there, waiting. Do not insert rows in the middle of "
        "a register.",
        "Filter a register rather than re-sorting it. The automatic "
        "IDs come from the row number, so re-sorting changes them.",
        "Nothing here needs macros, add-ins or a subscription. It "
        "opens in Excel 2010 and later, and in LibreOffice.",
        "Keep a backup. It is one file, and one file is one thing to "
        "lose.",
    ], LAST_COL, height=28)
    row += 1

    for note, height in [(ROUTE_NOTE, 56), (HONESTY_NOTE, 62),
                         (PAYROLL_NOTE, 52), (WEATHER_NOTE, 44),
                         (PHOTO_NOTE, 44), (PRIVACY_NOTE, 52),
                         (DISCLAIMER, 72)]:
        row = s.note(row, note, LAST_COL, height=height)
        row += 1

    s.finish(freeze_row=4, gridlines=False, fit_width=True)
    s.protect()
    return s
````

---

## 5. Customer, property, service

Workflow one, and the decision the rest of the product rests on. The CRM mints a Customer ID; the Property Database mints a Property ID and hangs it off that customer, with the lawn area, the drive time, the gate code and the dog; the Service Catalog prices the work per 1,000 sq ft, per visit, per hour or per plant, and carries the minutes each unit really takes. Everything after this joins back through those IDs.
