> Material purchases and material usage are both recorded here, which
> is how the materials tab knows what is in the yard without anybody
> counting it.

| Defines | What it does |
|---------|--------------|
| `spec(cfg)` |  |

**Module constants:** `NAME` ('Expense Tracker').

````python
"""18. Expense Tracker - everything that goes out, and what it belongs to.

Two kinds of line live here and the difference matters. A line with a
Job ID is a cost of doing that job, and it lands on that job's actual
cost and its profit. A line without one is a cost of being in
business - insurance, the accountant, the yard rent - and it belongs
to the month, not to any visit.

Material purchases and material usage are both recorded here, which
is how the materials tab knows what is in the yard without anybody
counting it.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from ..config import Palette, Config
from ..layout import Col, SheetSpec
from ..sample_data import get as sample
from ..writer import date_check, list_from
from .common import (alert_cf, alert_chain, amount, date_num_col, id_col,
                     is_job_cost, money_cf, month_col, pick,
                     yesno, zero)

NAME = "Expense Tracker"


def spec(cfg: Config) -> SheetSpec:
    return SheetSpec(
        name=NAME,
        title="Expense Tracker",
        subtitle="Every cost: the ones that belong to a job, and the "
                 "ones that belong to the business.",
        tab_color=Palette.deep,
        rows=cfg.row_count("expenses"),
        samples=sample("expenses", cfg),
        freeze_cols=3,
        help_text=(
            "One line per receipt. Put a Job ID on it only if the "
            "cost belongs to that visit - mulch delivered for it, "
            "the tip fee for its waste. Leave it blank for insurance, "
            "rent, advertising and everything else that is the cost "
            "of being open."),
        extra_notes=[
            "Booking a material purchase here with a Material ID and "
            "a quantity is what fills the Materials Inventory. Mark "
            "it as a purchase when it comes into the yard and as "
            "usage when it goes out to a job - the same tab, the "
            "'Purchase or usage' column apart.",
            "Do not re-enter fuel from the Vehicle & Fuel Tracker. "
            "It is already counted there, and the monthly reports "
            "add the two together.",
            "The tax category column is there to make your "
            "accountant's year-end quicker. It is a label for your "
            "own records, not tax advice, and what is deductible "
            "depends on where you trade.",
        ],
        columns=[
            id_col("date", '"EXP-"', digits=5, header="Line"),
            Col("date", "Date", 11, "date",
                validate=date_check(),
                note="Start here."),
            Col("supplier", "Paid to", 22, "text",
                note="Who got the money."),
            Col("description", "What for", 30, "wrap"),
            Col("category", "Category", 22, "text",
                validate=list_from("ExpenseCategoryList"),
                note="Four of these - materials, fuel, subcontract "
                     "and disposal - count as job costs when a Job ID "
                     "is attached."),
            Col("amount", "Amount", 11, "money",
                validate=amount(0, 1000000),
                cf=money_cf(),
                note="What you paid, including tax if you cannot "
                     "reclaim it."),
            Col("tax_amount", "Tax paid", 10, "money",
                validate=amount(0, 1000000),
                note="The tax portion, if you are registered and can "
                     "reclaim it."),
            Col("job", "Job ID", 11, "id",
                validate=pick("JobIDList",
                              "Pick the visit this cost belongs to."),
                note="Only when the cost belongs to one visit. This is "
                     "what makes job profit real rather than "
                     "theoretical."),
            Col("job_address", "Where", 24, "text",
                formula='=IF({job}{r}="","",'
                        'IFERROR(INDEX('
                        '[[Job & Work Orders.address]],'
                        'MATCH({job}{r},'
                        '[[Job & Work Orders.id]],0))&"",'
                        '"Check - Job ID not found"))',
                cf=[{"type": "text", "criteria": "containing",
                     "value": "Check -", "fmt": "bad"}]),
            Col("material", "Material ID", 11, "id",
                validate=pick("MaterialIDList",
                              "Only for material purchases and usage. "
                              "Pick from the Materials Inventory."),
                note="Fills the stock figures on the Materials tab."),
            Col("movement", "Purchase or usage", 15, "text",
                validate=pick("StockMoveList",
                              "Is this material coming into the yard "
                              "or going out to a job?"),
                note="'Purchase' adds to stock; 'Usage' and "
                     "'Wastage' both take it away. Leave blank for "
                     "anything that is not a material."),
            Col("quantity", "Quantity", 10, "number1",
                validate=amount(0, 1000000,
                                "How many units, in the material's own "
                                "unit."),
                note="In the unit set on the Materials tab - bags, "
                     "yards, gallons."),
            Col("equipment", "Equipment ID", 11, "id",
                validate=pick("EquipmentIDList",
                              "For repairs, servicing and parts - "
                              "which machine was it for?"),
                note="Builds each machine's running cost and tells you "
                     "when repairing stops being worth it."),
            Col("vehicle", "Vehicle", 14, "text",
                validate=pick("VehicleList",
                              "For vehicle costs not already on the "
                              "fuel tracker."),
                note="Use the Vehicle & Fuel Tracker for fuel. This is "
                     "for anything else."),
            Col("payment_method", "Paid by", 15, "text",
                validate=list_from("PaymentMethodList")),
            Col("reference", "Receipt no.", 13, "text",
                note="Where to find the paperwork in six months."),
            Col("tax_category", "Tax category", 18, "text",
                validate=list_from("TaxCategoryList"),
                note="A label for your accountant. Not tax advice."),
            Col("recurring", "Recurring", 10, "flag",
                validate=yesno("Does this come round every month?"),
                note="Insurance, rent, software. Worth knowing what "
                     "your fixed monthly floor is."),
            Col("billable", "Rebill?", 9, "flag",
                validate=yesno("Will you charge this on to the "
                               "customer?"),
                note="Rebilled costs should appear as extras on the "
                     "job, otherwise you have paid for them twice."),
            Col("receipt_link", "Receipt link", 18, "link",
                note="A link to the photo of the receipt in your cloud "
                     "drive."),
            Col("notes", "Notes", 24, "wrap"),

            # ---- the flags other tabs count on -----------------------
            Col("is_job_cost", "Job cost", 8, "int",
                formula=zero("date",
                             'IF(AND({job}{r}<>"",'
                             f'{is_job_cost("{category}{r}")}),1,0)'),
                hidden=True,
                note="A job cost is one with a Job ID AND a category "
                     "that belongs to a job."),
            Col("is_overhead", "Overhead", 8, "int",
                formula=zero("date",
                             'IF({is_job_cost}{r}=1,0,1)'),
                hidden=True),
            Col("is_purchase", "Purchase", 8, "int",
                formula=zero("date",
                             'IF(AND({material}{r}<>"",'
                             '{movement}{r}="Purchase",'
                             '{quantity}{r}>0),1,0)'),
                hidden=True),
            Col("is_usage", "Usage", 7, "int",
                formula=zero("date",
                             'IF(AND({material}{r}<>"",'
                             '({movement}{r}="Usage")+'
                             '({movement}{r}="Wastage")>0,'
                             '{quantity}{r}>0),1,0)'),
                hidden=True),
            Col("unit_cost", "Cost per unit", 11, "money",
                formula='=IF({date}{r}="","",'
                        'IF({quantity}{r}<=0,"",'
                        'IF({is_purchase}{r}=0,"",'
                        'ROUND({amount}{r}/'
                        '{quantity}{r},2))))',
                note="What this delivery actually cost per unit. "
                     "Compare it with the Materials tab - prices "
                     "move."),
            date_num_col("date", "date_num"),
            month_col("date"),

            Col("alert", "What needs doing", 44, "wrap",
                formula=alert_chain("date", [
                    ('{job_address}{r}="Check - Job ID not found"',
                     "Check - the Job ID on this line does not exist"),
                    ('{amount}{r}=0',
                     "Add - no amount on this line"),
                    ('AND({material}{r}<>"",{movement}{r}="")',
                     "Add - material with no purchase or usage set, so "
                     "stock is not moving"),
                    ('AND({material}{r}<>"",{quantity}{r}=0)',
                     "Add - material with no quantity"),
                    ('AND({category}{r}="Materials",{job}{r}="",'
                     '{material}{r}="")',
                     "Check - materials cost tied to neither a job nor "
                     "a material"),
                    ('{category}{r}=""',
                     "Add - no category, so it will not reach the "
                     "right report"),
                    ('AND({billable}{r}="Yes",{job}{r}="")',
                     "Check - marked to rebill but no job to rebill "
                     "it on"),
                    ('{reference}{r}=""',
                     "Add - no receipt reference"),
                ]),
                cf=alert_cf()),
        ],
    )
````

### `landscaping_spreadsheet/sheets/feedback.py`

*180 lines · 1 function*

> 19. Customer Feedback - what they said, and what you did about it.
>
> Complaints are not the enemy; unrecorded complaints are. A row here
> with a date, a cause and a resolution is a business learning
> something. The same complaint arriving three times from three
> customers, with nobody noticing, is a business about to lose them.
>
> Scores feed back to the customer record and to the crew scorecard, so
> one piece of feedback lands everywhere it is relevant.

| Defines | What it does |
|---------|--------------|
| `spec(cfg)` |  |

**Module constants:** `NAME` ('Customer Feedback').

````python
"""19. Customer Feedback - what they said, and what you did about it.

Complaints are not the enemy; unrecorded complaints are. A row here
with a date, a cause and a resolution is a business learning
something. The same complaint arriving three times from three
customers, with nobody noticing, is a business about to lose them.

Scores feed back to the customer record and to the crew scorecard, so
one piece of feedback lands everywhere it is relevant.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from ..config import Palette, Config
from ..layout import Col, SheetSpec
from ..sample_data import get as sample
from ..writer import date_check, list_from
from .common import (alert_cf, alert_chain, amount, customer_col,
                     date_num_col, id_col, lookup_customer, month_col,
                     pick, rating_cf, score_validation,
                     status_cf, yesno, zero)

NAME = "Customer Feedback"


def spec(cfg: Config) -> SheetSpec:
    return SheetSpec(
        name=NAME,
        title="Customer Feedback",
        subtitle="Compliments, complaints and scores - with the cause, "
                 "the fix and how long it took.",
        tab_color=Palette.primary_mid,
        rows=cfg.row_count("feedback"),
        samples=sample("feedback", cfg),
        freeze_cols=3,
        help_text=(
            "Log everything, good and bad. Put the Job ID on it when "
            "it is about a particular visit, and the score feeds both "
            "the customer's record and the crew scorecard. Then fill "
            "in what you did about it - that column is the one that "
            "turns a complaint into a repeat customer."),
        extra_notes=[
            "A score of 1 to 5. Anything at or below 3 counts as a "
            "problem and is chased on the dashboard until it is "
            "resolved.",
            "Recording the cause honestly - rushed, wrong day, "
            "machine broke, price surprise - is how three "
            "complaints turn into one fix.",
            "Ask for a review only after a resolved complaint or a "
            "compliment. The 'Review asked' column stops you asking "
            "the same person twice.",
        ],
        columns=[
            id_col("date", '"FBK-"', digits=4, header="Line"),
            Col("date", "Date", 11, "date",
                validate=date_check(),
                note="Start here - the day they told you."),
            customer_col(),
            Col("customer_name", "Customer", 20, "text",
                formula=lookup_customer("name")),
            Col("job", "Job ID", 11, "id",
                validate=pick("JobIDList",
                              "Pick the visit this is about."),
                note="If it is about one visit. Blank if it is general."),
            Col("property", "Property ID", 11, "id",
                validate=pick("PropertyList",
                              "Where it happened."),
                note="Where it happened, if it matters."),
            Col("crew", "Crew", 12, "text",
                validate=list_from("CrewNameList"),
                note="Which crew the feedback is about. Feeds their "
                     "quality score."),
            Col("source", "How it came in", 16, "text",
                validate=list_from("FeedbackSourceList")),
            Col("type", "Type", 16, "text",
                validate=list_from("FeedbackTypeList"),
                cf=status_cf()),
            Col("score", "Score", 8, "rating",
                validate=score_validation(5),
                cf=rating_cf(),
                note="1 to 5. Three or below is treated as a problem."),
            Col("comment", "What they said", 38, "wrap",
                note="Their words, not your summary of them. They are "
                     "more useful later."),
            Col("cause", "Cause", 20, "text",
                validate=pick("FeedbackCauseList",
                              "Why did it happen? Be honest - this is "
                              "for you, not for them."),
                note="The pattern in this column is worth more than "
                     "any individual complaint."),
            Col("action", "What you did", 34, "wrap",
                note="The fix. 'Recut the front, apologised, no "
                     "charge' is a complete answer."),
            Col("cost", "Cost to fix", 11, "money",
                validate=amount(0, 1000000,
                                "What putting it right cost you - a "
                                "free visit, a refund, replacement "
                                "plants."),
                note="Worth knowing. Poor work is rarely free."),
            Col("status", "Status", 14, "text",
                validate=list_from("FeedbackStatusList"),
                cf=status_cf()),
            Col("resolved_date", "Resolved on", 12, "date",
                validate=date_check()),
            Col("owner", "Handled by", 16, "text"),
            Col("review_asked", "Review asked", 11, "flag",
                validate=yesno("Have you asked this customer for a "
                               "public review?"),
                note="Ask after a compliment or a resolved complaint, "
                     "never after an unresolved one."),
            Col("review_left", "Review left", 10, "flag",
                validate=yesno()),
            Col("follow_up", "Follow up on", 12, "date",
                validate=date_check(),
                note="Put a date here and it will be chased."),
            Col("notes", "Notes", 24, "wrap"),

            # ---- the worked out bits ---------------------------------
            Col("is_problem", "Problem", 8, "int",
                formula=zero("date",
                             'IF(({type}{r}="Complaint")+'
                             '({type}{r}="Quality issue")+'
                             '({type}{r}="Damage claim")+'
                             '(AND({score}{r}>0,{score}{r}<=3))>0,'
                             '1,0)'),
                hidden=True,
                note="A complaint, a quality issue, a damage claim or "
                     "a score of three or less."),
            Col("is_open", "Open", 6, "int",
                formula=zero("date",
                             'IF(({status}{r}="Resolved")+'
                             '({status}{r}="Closed")>0,0,1)'),
                hidden=True),
            Col("days_open", "Days open", 10, "int",
                formula=zero("date",
                             'IF({is_open}{r}=0,'
                             'IF({resolved_date}{r}="",0,'
                             '{resolved_date}{r}-{date}{r}),'
                             'TodayDate-{date}{r})'),
                cf=[{"type": "cell", "criteria": ">=", "value": 14,
                     "fmt": "bad"},
                    {"type": "cell", "criteria": ">=", "value": 7,
                     "fmt": "warn"}],
                note="How long it took, or how long it has been "
                     "sitting there."),
            Col("is_promoter", "Promoter", 8, "int",
                formula=zero("date",
                             'IF({score}{r}>=4,1,0)'),
                hidden=True),
            date_num_col("date", "date_num"),
            month_col("date"),

            Col("alert", "What needs doing", 44, "wrap",
                formula=alert_chain("date", [
                    ('AND({is_problem}{r}=1,{is_open}{r}=1,'
                     '{days_open}{r}>=7)',
                     "Urgent - an unresolved complaint over a week old"),
                    ('AND({is_problem}{r}=1,{is_open}{r}=1)',
                     "Action - complaint open, deal with it today"),
                    ('AND({is_open}{r}=1,{follow_up}{r}<>"",'
                     '{follow_up}{r}<=TodayDate)',
                     "Follow up - the follow-up date has arrived"),
                    ('AND({is_problem}{r}=1,{cause}{r}="")',
                     "Add - no cause recorded, so nothing can be "
                     "learned from it"),
                    ('AND({is_problem}{r}=1,{action}{r}="")',
                     "Add - nothing recorded about what you did"),
                    ('AND({is_promoter}{r}=1,'
                     '{review_asked}{r}<>"Yes")',
                     "Action - a happy customer who has not been "
                     "asked for a review"),
                    ('AND({review_asked}{r}="Yes",'
                     '{review_left}{r}<>"Yes",'
                     'TodayDate-{date}{r}>=FeedbackChaseDays)',
                     "Follow up - review asked for but never left"),
                    ('{is_open}{r}=1', "Due - still open"),
                ]),
                cf=alert_cf()),
        ],
    )
````

### `landscaping_spreadsheet/sheets/profit.py`

*274 lines · 1 function*

> 21. Profit Analysis - the month-by-month truth.
>
> Fourteen rows, one per month, every figure calculated. Revenue from
> completed jobs, labour from the timesheets, materials and overheads
> from the expenses, fuel from the vehicle tracker - and what is left.
>
> The useful column is the one on the right: profit per crew hour.
> Revenue can grow while that number shrinks, and when it does you are
> working harder for less. No other single figure in this trade is
> worth as much attention.

| Defines | What it does |
|---------|--------------|
| `spec(cfg)` |  |

**Module constants:** `NAME` ('Profit Analysis').

````python
"""21. Profit Analysis - the month-by-month truth.

Fourteen rows, one per month, every figure calculated. Revenue from
completed jobs, labour from the timesheets, materials and overheads
from the expenses, fuel from the vehicle tracker - and what is left.

The useful column is the one on the right: profit per crew hour.
Revenue can grow while that number shrinks, and when it does you are
working harder for less. No other single figure in this trade is
worth as much attention.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from ..config import Palette, Config
from ..layout import Col, SheetSpec
from ..sample_data import get as sample
from .common import (alert_cf, alert_chain, loss_cf, money_cf, scale_cf,
                     zero)

NAME = "Profit Analysis"


def spec(cfg: Config) -> SheetSpec:
    return SheetSpec(
        name=NAME,
        title="Profit Analysis",
        subtitle="Month by month: what came in, what went out, what "
                 "was left, and what each crew hour earned.",
        tab_color=Palette.deep,
        rows=cfg.row_count("profit"),
        samples=sample("profit", cfg),
        freeze_cols=2,
        help_text=(
            "Choose a month in the first column and every figure on "
            "the row fills itself in from the registers. Nothing "
            "here is typed except the month and your own target. "
            "Fourteen rows gives you a full year plus the two months "
            "either side for comparison."),
        extra_notes=[
            "Revenue counts completed jobs only, excluding tax. Tax "
            "collected is never yours and does not belong in a "
            "profit figure.",
            "Labour is what the timesheets cost, including the hours "
            "that were not billable. Driving and yard time are real "
            "wages and leaving them out is the most common way a "
            "landscaping business flatters itself.",
            "Profit per crew hour is the number to watch across the "
            "season. If it falls while revenue rises, the extra work "
            "is not paying for itself.",
        ],
        columns=[
            Col("month", "Month", 11, "text",
                validate={"validate": "list", "source": "=MonthKeys",
                          "input_message": "Pick a month in yyyy-mm "
                                           "form."},
                note="Pick the month. Everything else follows."),
            Col("label", "Period", 14, "text",
                formula='=IF({month}{r}="","",'
                        'TEXT(DATE(VALUE(LEFT({month}{r},4)),'
                        'VALUE(RIGHT({month}{r},2)),1),'
                        '"mmmm yyyy"))'),
            Col("start_num", "From", 10, "number",
                formula=zero("month",
                             'DATE(VALUE(LEFT({month}{r},4)),'
                             'VALUE(RIGHT({month}{r},2)),1)'),
                hidden=True),
            Col("end_num", "To", 10, "number",
                formula=zero("month",
                             'DATE(VALUE(LEFT({month}{r},4)),'
                             'VALUE(RIGHT({month}{r},2))+1,1)-1'),
                hidden=True),

            # ---- money in --------------------------------------------
            Col("jobs_done", "Visits done", 11, "int",
                formula=zero("month",
                             'COUNTIFS([[Job & Work Orders.month]],'
                             '{month}{r},'
                             '[[Job & Work Orders.is_done]],1)'),
                cf=scale_cf()),
            Col("revenue", "Revenue", 12, "money",
                formula=zero("month",
                             'ROUND(SUMIFS('
                             '[[Job & Work Orders.charge]],'
                             '[[Job & Work Orders.month]],{month}{r},'
                             '[[Job & Work Orders.is_done]],1)+'
                             'SUMIFS([[Job & Work Orders.extras]],'
                             '[[Job & Work Orders.month]],{month}{r},'
                             '[[Job & Work Orders.is_done]],1),2)'),
                cf=money_cf(),
                note="Completed visits, excluding tax."),
            Col("invoiced", "Invoiced", 12, "money",
                formula=zero("month",
                             'SUMIFS([[Invoice & Payments.total]],'
                             '[[Invoice & Payments.month]],'
                             '{month}{r})'),
                note="What you billed in the month, which is not the "
                     "same thing as what you earned in it."),
            Col("collected", "Collected", 12, "money",
                formula=zero("month",
                             'SUMIFS([[Invoice & Payments.paid]],'
                             '[[Invoice & Payments.month]],'
                             '{month}{r})'),
                note="What arrived against invoices raised that "
                     "month. Cash, as opposed to earnings."),

            # ---- money out -------------------------------------------
            Col("labour", "Labour", 11, "money",
                formula=zero("month",
                             'SUMIFS([[Timesheets & Payroll.cost]],'
                             '[[Timesheets & Payroll.month]],'
                             '{month}{r})'),
                note="Every hour on the timesheets, billable or not."),
            Col("materials", "Materials", 11, "money",
                formula=zero("month",
                             'SUMIFS([[Expense Tracker.amount]],'
                             '[[Expense Tracker.month]],{month}{r},'
                             '[[Expense Tracker.category]],'
                             '"Materials")'),
                note="Bought in the month, from the expense tracker."),
            Col("fuel", "Fuel & vehicles", 13, "money",
                formula=zero("month",
                             'SUMIFS('
                             '[[Vehicle & Fuel Tracker.amount]],'
                             '[[Vehicle & Fuel Tracker.month]],'
                             '{month}{r})'),
                note="Everything on the vehicle tracker - fuel, "
                     "repairs, tax and insurance."),
            Col("other_costs", "Other costs", 12, "money",
                formula=zero("month",
                             'ROUND(SUMIFS('
                             '[[Expense Tracker.amount]],'
                             '[[Expense Tracker.month]],{month}{r})-'
                             'SUMIFS([[Expense Tracker.amount]],'
                             '[[Expense Tracker.month]],{month}{r},'
                             '[[Expense Tracker.category]],'
                             '"Materials"),2)'),
                note="Everything else on the expense tracker - "
                     "insurance, rent, advertising, the accountant."),
            Col("total_costs", "Total costs", 12, "money",
                formula=zero("month",
                             'ROUND({labour}{r}+{materials}{r}+'
                             '{fuel}{r}+{other_costs}{r},2)')),

            # ---- what is left ----------------------------------------
            Col("profit", "Profit", 12, "money",
                formula=zero("month",
                             'ROUND({revenue}{r}-{total_costs}{r},2)'),
                cf=loss_cf(),
                note="Revenue less everything. The number that pays "
                     "you."),
            Col("margin_pct", "Margin", 9, "percent",
                formula='=IF({month}{r}="","",'
                        'IF({revenue}{r}<=0,"",'
                        'ROUND({profit}{r}/'
                        '{revenue}{r},4)))',
                cf=[{"type": "cell", "criteria": ">=",
                     "value": "=TargetMargin", "fmt": "good"},
                    {"type": "cell", "criteria": "<", "value": 0,
                     "fmt": "bold_bad"},
                    {"type": "cell", "criteria": "<",
                     "value": "=MinMargin", "fmt": "bad"}]),
            Col("hours", "Crew hours", 11, "number1",
                formula=zero("month",
                             'SUMIFS([[Timesheets & Payroll.hours]],'
                             '[[Timesheets & Payroll.month]],'
                             '{month}{r})')),
            Col("billable_hours", "Billable hrs", 11, "number1",
                formula=zero("month",
                             'SUMIFS([[Timesheets & Payroll.hours]],'
                             '[[Timesheets & Payroll.month]],'
                             '{month}{r},'
                             '[[Timesheets & Payroll.is_billable]],'
                             '1)')),
            Col("billable_pct", "Billable %", 10, "percent",
                formula='=IF({month}{r}="","",'
                        'IF({hours}{r}<=0,"",'
                        'ROUND({billable_hours}{r}/'
                        '{hours}{r},4)))',
                cf=[{"type": "cell", "criteria": ">=", "value": 0.75,
                     "fmt": "good"},
                    {"type": "cell", "criteria": "<", "value": 0.55,
                     "fmt": "bad"}],
                note="How much of the paid day was sold. The quiet "
                     "killer in this trade."),
            Col("revenue_hour", "Revenue / hour", 12, "money",
                formula='=IF({month}{r}="","",'
                        'IF({hours}{r}<=0,"",'
                        'ROUND({revenue}{r}/'
                        '{hours}{r},2)))',
                cf=[{"type": "cell", "criteria": ">=",
                     "value": "=ChargeRate", "fmt": "good"},
                    {"type": "cell", "criteria": "<",
                     "value": "=ChargeRate", "fmt": "warn"}]),
            Col("profit_hour", "Profit / hour", 12, "money",
                formula='=IF({month}{r}="","",'
                        'IF({hours}{r}<=0,"",'
                        'ROUND({profit}{r}/'
                        '{hours}{r},2)))',
                cf=loss_cf(),
                note="The single most useful number on the tab. Watch "
                     "it across a season."),
            Col("avg_job", "Average visit", 12, "money",
                formula='=IF({month}{r}="","",'
                        'IF({jobs_done}{r}<=0,"",'
                        'ROUND({revenue}{r}/'
                        '{jobs_done}{r},2)))'),
            Col("new_customers", "New customers", 12, "int",
                formula=zero("month",
                             'COUNTIFS([[Customer CRM.first_job]],'
                             '">="&{start_num}{r},'
                             '[[Customer CRM.first_job]],'
                             '"<="&{end_num}{r})')),
            Col("target", "Your target", 11, "money",
                validate={"validate": "decimal", "criteria": "between",
                          "minimum": 0, "maximum": 10000000,
                          "error_title": "Monthly target",
                          "error_message": "What you want this month "
                                           "to bring in. Blank uses "
                                           "the yearly target divided "
                                           "by twelve."},
                note="Your number for this month. Blank falls back to "
                     "a twelfth of the annual target on Settings."),
            Col("target_used", "Target used", 11, "money",
                formula=zero("month",
                             'IF({target}{r}>0,{target}{r},'
                             'ROUND(RevenueTarget/12,2))'),
                hidden=True),
            Col("vs_target", "vs target", 11, "money",
                formula=zero("month",
                             'ROUND({revenue}{r}-'
                             '{target_used}{r},2)'),
                cf=loss_cf()),
            Col("target_pct", "% of target", 10, "percent",
                formula='=IF({month}{r}="","",'
                        'IF({target_used}{r}<=0,"",'
                        'ROUND({revenue}{r}/'
                        '{target_used}{r},4)))',
                cf=[{"type": "cell", "criteria": ">=", "value": 1,
                     "fmt": "good"},
                    {"type": "cell", "criteria": "<", "value": 0.8,
                     "fmt": "bad"}]),
            Col("notes", "Notes", 30, "wrap",
                note="Why the month went the way it did. 'Three weeks "
                     "of rain' explains a lot, a year later."),

            Col("alert", "What it is telling you", 44, "wrap",
                formula=alert_chain("month", [
                    ('{profit}{r}<0',
                     "Loss - this month cost more than it earned"),
                    ('AND({revenue}{r}>0,{margin_pct}{r}<>"",'
                     '{margin_pct}{r}<MinMargin)',
                     "Urgent - margin below your floor"),
                    ('AND({hours}{r}>0,{billable_pct}{r}<>"",'
                     '{billable_pct}{r}<0.55)',
                     "Check - too much of the paid day was not sold"),
                    ('AND({revenue}{r}>0,{target_pct}{r}<>"",'
                     '{target_pct}{r}<0.8)',
                     "Action - well under target for the month"),
                    ('AND({revenue}{r}>0,{collected}{r}<'
                     '{invoiced}{r}*0.7)',
                     "Chase - a lot of what you invoiced is still "
                     "out"),
                    ('AND({revenue}{r}>0,{margin_pct}{r}<>"",'
                     '{margin_pct}{r}<TargetMargin)',
                     "Thin margin - under target, above the floor"),
                    ('AND({revenue}{r}=0,{month}{r}<>"")',
                     "Add - no completed work recorded this month"),
                    ('AND({target_pct}{r}<>"",{target_pct}{r}>=1)',
                     "OK - target met"),
                ]),
                cf=alert_cf()),
        ],
    )
````

### `landscaping_spreadsheet/sheets/monthly.py`

*289 lines · 2 functions*

> `Monthly Reports` - one month, read properly.
>
> Pick a month at the top and everything below it changes: the money,
> the work, the crew, the customers who grew and the ones who went
> quiet. It is the fifteen minutes at the end of a month that tells you
> whether the last four weeks were worth doing.

| Defines | What it does |
|---------|--------------|
| `_month_sum(name_range, month_range)` |  |
| `build(book, fmts, cfg, reg)` |  |

**Module constants:** `NAME` ('Monthly Reports'), `LAST_COL` (11), `LIST_ROWS` (8).

````python
"""`Monthly Reports` - one month, read properly.

Pick a month at the top and everything below it changes: the money,
the work, the crew, the customers who grew and the ones who went
quiet. It is the fifteen minutes at the end of a month that tells you
whether the last four weeks were worth doing.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from ..config import Config, Palette
from ..layout import Registry
from ..writer import SheetWriter

NAME = "Monthly Reports"
LAST_COL = 11
LIST_ROWS = 8


def _month_sum(name_range: str, month_range: str) -> str:
    return f"SUMIFS({name_range},{month_range},ReportMonth)"


def build(book, fmts, cfg: Config, reg: Registry) -> SheetWriter:
    s = SheetWriter(book, fmts, cfg, reg, NAME,
                    tab_color=Palette.primary_mid)

    s.banner("Monthly Reports",
             "Pick a month. The whole page follows - money, work, "
             "crew, customers and what to do about them.",
             LAST_COL)
    s.set_col(0, 0, 26)
    for column in range(1, LAST_COL + 1):
        s.set_col(column, column, 14)

    row = 4
    row = s.field(row, "Month to report on", None, style="text",
                  name="ReportMonth", mode="input",
                  formula='=TEXT(TodayDate,"yyyy-mm")',
                  help_col=5,
                  help_text="Defaults to this month. Type any "
                            "yyyy-mm to look back.")
    s.ws.data_validation(row - 1, 3, row - 1, 3,
                         {"validate": "list", "source": "=MonthKeys",
                          "input_message": "Pick a month in yyyy-mm "
                                           "form, for example "
                                           "2026-07."})
    row += 1
    row = s.note(
        row,
        "The month cell above is the only thing you change on this "
        "page. Everything else is calculated from the registers, so "
        "a report for March is never out of date - it is rebuilt "
        "the moment you ask for it.",
        LAST_COL, height=30)
    row += 1

    # ----------------------------------------------------------- money --
    row = s.section(row, "The money", LAST_COL)
    s.kpi(row, 0, "Revenue",
          '=SUMIFS(JobCharge,JobMonth,ReportMonth,JobIsDone,1)+'
          'SUMIFS(JobExtras,JobMonth,ReportMonth,JobIsDone,1)',
          "money", note="Completed visits, excluding tax")
    s.kpi(row, 2, "Invoiced", f'={_month_sum("InvTotal", "InvMonth")}',
          "money", note="What you billed in the month")
    s.kpi(row, 4, "Collected", f'={_month_sum("InvPaid", "InvMonth")}',
          "money", note="What actually arrived")
    s.kpi(row, 6, "Labour cost",
          f'={_month_sum("TimeCost", "TimeMonth")}', "money",
          note="Every hour, billable or not")
    s.kpi(row, 8, "Other costs",
          f'={_month_sum("ExpAmount", "ExpMonth")}+'
          f'{_month_sum("VehAmount", "VehMonth")}', "money",
          note="Expenses and vehicles")
    row = s.kpi(row, 10, "Profit",
                '=SUMIFS(JobCharge,JobMonth,ReportMonth,JobIsDone,1)+'
                'SUMIFS(JobExtras,JobMonth,ReportMonth,JobIsDone,1)-'
                f'{_month_sum("TimeCost", "TimeMonth")}-'
                f'{_month_sum("ExpAmount", "ExpMonth")}-'
                f'{_month_sum("VehAmount", "VehMonth")}',
                "money", note="What was left")
    row += 1

    # ------------------------------------------------------------ work --
    row = s.section(row, "The work", LAST_COL)
    s.kpi(row, 0, "Visits completed",
          '=COUNTIFS(JobMonth,ReportMonth,JobIsDone,1)', "int")
    s.kpi(row, 2, "Visits cancelled",
          '=COUNTIFS(JobMonth,ReportMonth,JobStatus,"Cancelled")',
          "int", note="Worth watching the reason")
    s.kpi(row, 4, "Rained off",
          '=COUNTIFS(JobMonth,ReportMonth,JobStatus,"Rained off")',
          "int")
    s.kpi(row, 6, "Crew hours",
          f'={_month_sum("TimeHours", "TimeMonth")}', "number1")
    s.kpi(row, 8, "Billable hours",
          '=SUMIFS(TimeHours,TimeMonth,ReportMonth,TimeIsBillable,1)',
          "number1")
    row = s.kpi(row, 10, "Billable share",
                '=IFERROR(SUMIFS(TimeHours,TimeMonth,ReportMonth,'
                'TimeIsBillable,1)/'
                f'{_month_sum("TimeHours", "TimeMonth")},"")',
                "percent0", note="Share of paid time that was sold")
    row += 1

    # ---------------------------------------------------- per hour --
    row = s.section(row, "What an hour was worth", LAST_COL)
    s.kpi(row, 0, "Revenue per crew hour",
          '=IFERROR((SUMIFS(JobCharge,JobMonth,ReportMonth,'
          'JobIsDone,1)+SUMIFS(JobExtras,JobMonth,ReportMonth,'
          f'JobIsDone,1))/{_month_sum("TimeHours", "TimeMonth")},"")',
          "money", note="Against your charge-out rate")
    s.kpi(row, 2, "Average visit",
          '=IFERROR(SUMIFS(JobCharge,JobMonth,ReportMonth,JobIsDone,1)'
          '/COUNTIFS(JobMonth,ReportMonth,JobIsDone,1),"")', "money")
    s.kpi(row, 4, "Quotes sent",
          '=COUNTIFS(QuoteMonth,ReportMonth)', "int")
    s.kpi(row, 6, "Quotes won",
          '=COUNTIFS(QuoteMonth,ReportMonth,QuoteIsWon,1)', "int")
    s.kpi(row, 8, "Win rate",
          '=IFERROR(COUNTIFS(QuoteMonth,ReportMonth,QuoteIsWon,1)/'
          'COUNTIFS(QuoteMonth,ReportMonth),"")', "percent0")
    row = s.kpi(row, 10, "New customers",
                '=COUNTIFS(CustFirstJob,">="&DATE(VALUE(LEFT('
                'ReportMonth,4)),VALUE(RIGHT(ReportMonth,2)),1),'
                'CustFirstJob,"<="&DATE(VALUE(LEFT(ReportMonth,4)),'
                'VALUE(RIGHT(ReportMonth,2))+1,1)-1)', "int")
    row += 1

    # ----------------------------------------------------- money owed --
    row = s.section(row, "Money still out there", LAST_COL)
    row = s.note(
        row,
        "Aged from the due date, not the invoice date, so an "
        "invoice on thirty day terms is not counted late the "
        "moment it is sent. These four buckets cover every unpaid "
        "invoice in the workbook, not only this month's.",
        LAST_COL, height=32)
    row += 1
    s.kpi(row, 0, "Not due yet",
          '=SUMIFS(InvOutstanding,InvAgeBand,"Not due yet")', "money")
    s.kpi(row, 2, "1-30 days",
          '=SUMIFS(InvOutstanding,InvAgeBand,"1-30 days")', "money")
    s.kpi(row, 4, "31-60 days",
          '=SUMIFS(InvOutstanding,InvAgeBand,"31-60 days")', "money")
    s.kpi(row, 6, "61-90 days",
          '=SUMIFS(InvOutstanding,InvAgeBand,"61-90 days")', "money")
    s.kpi(row, 8, "Over 90 days",
          '=SUMIFS(InvOutstanding,InvAgeBand,"Over 90 days")',
          "money", note="Decide: chase, settle or write off")
    row = s.kpi(row, 10, "Total owed", "=SUM(InvOutstanding)",
                "money")
    row += 2

    # ------------------------------------------------ top services --
    row = s.section(row, "What sold this month", LAST_COL)
    s.table_head(row, 0,
                 ["Service", "Visits", "Revenue", "Avg price",
                  "Share of month"],
                 widths=[30, 11, 13, 13, 14])
    head = row
    for n in range(1, LIST_ROWS + 1):
        line = head + n
        pick = ('IFERROR(INDEX([[Service Catalog.name]],'
                f'MATCH(ROW()-{head + 1},'
                '[[Service Catalog.revenue_rank]],0)),"")')
        s.formula(line, 0, "=" + pick, fmts.calc("text"))
        s.formula(line, 1,
                  f'=IF($A{line + 1}="","",'
                  f'COUNTIFS(JobService,$A{line + 1},JobMonth,'
                  'ReportMonth,JobIsDone,1))', fmts.calc("int"))
        s.formula(line, 2,
                  f'=IF($A{line + 1}="","",'
                  f'SUMIFS(JobCharge,JobService,$A{line + 1},'
                  'JobMonth,ReportMonth,JobIsDone,1))',
                  fmts.calc("money"))
        s.formula(line, 3,
                  f'=IF($B{line + 1}="","",IF($B{line + 1}=0,"",'
                  f'ROUND($C{line + 1}/$B{line + 1},2)))',
                  fmts.calc("money"))
        s.formula(line, 4,
                  f'=IFERROR($C{line + 1}/'
                  'SUMIFS(JobCharge,JobMonth,ReportMonth,JobIsDone,1)'
                  ',"")', fmts.calc("percent"))
    row = head + LIST_ROWS + 2

    # ------------------------------------------------- top customers --
    row = s.section(row, "Who spent the most this month", LAST_COL)
    s.table_head(row, 0,
                 ["Customer", "Visits", "Revenue", "Owing",
                  "Last visit"],
                 widths=[28, 11, 13, 13, 13])
    head = row
    for n in range(1, LIST_ROWS + 1):
        line = head + n
        rank = f"ROW()-{head + 1}"
        s.formula(line, 0,
                  '=IFERROR(INDEX([[Customer CRM.name]],'
                  f'MATCH({rank},[[Customer CRM.lifetime_rank]],0)),'
                  '"")', fmts.calc("text"))
        s.formula(line, 1,
                  '=IFERROR(COUNTIFS(JobCustomer,'
                  f'INDEX([[Customer CRM.id]],MATCH({rank},'
                  '[[Customer CRM.lifetime_rank]],0)),JobMonth,'
                  'ReportMonth,JobIsDone,1),"")', fmts.calc("int"))
        s.formula(line, 2,
                  '=IFERROR(SUMIFS(JobCharge,JobCustomer,'
                  f'INDEX([[Customer CRM.id]],MATCH({rank},'
                  '[[Customer CRM.lifetime_rank]],0)),JobMonth,'
                  'ReportMonth,JobIsDone,1),"")', fmts.calc("money"))
        s.formula(line, 3,
                  '=IFERROR(INDEX([[Customer CRM.owing]],'
                  f'MATCH({rank},[[Customer CRM.lifetime_rank]],0))*1,'
                  '"")', fmts.calc("money"))
        s.formula(line, 4,
                  '=IFERROR(IF(INDEX([[Customer CRM.last_job]],'
                  f'MATCH({rank},[[Customer CRM.lifetime_rank]],0))='
                  '"","",INDEX([[Customer CRM.last_job]],'
                  f'MATCH({rank},[[Customer CRM.lifetime_rank]],0))),'
                  '"")', fmts.calc("date"))
    row = head + LIST_ROWS + 2

    # --------------------------------------------------- the crew --
    row = s.section(row, "The crew this month", LAST_COL)
    s.table_head(row, 0,
                 ["Name", "Hours", "Billable", "Billable %", "Cost",
                  "Overtime"],
                 widths=[24, 11, 11, 12, 12, 11])
    head = row
    for n in range(1, LIST_ROWS + 1):
        line = head + n
        rank = f"ROW()-{head + 1}"
        who = (f'INDEX([[Crew Database.id]],MATCH({rank},'
               '[[Crew Database.revenue_rank]],0))')
        s.formula(line, 0,
                  '=IFERROR(INDEX([[Crew Database.name]],'
                  f'MATCH({rank},[[Crew Database.revenue_rank]],0)),'
                  '"")', fmts.calc("text"))
        s.formula(line, 1,
                  f'=IFERROR(SUMIFS(TimeHours,TimeEmployee,{who},'
                  'TimeMonth,ReportMonth),"")', fmts.calc("number1"))
        s.formula(line, 2,
                  f'=IFERROR(SUMIFS(TimeHours,TimeEmployee,{who},'
                  'TimeMonth,ReportMonth,TimeIsBillable,1),"")',
                  fmts.calc("number1"))
        s.formula(line, 3,
                  f'=IF($B{line + 1}="","",IF($B{line + 1}=0,"",'
                  f'ROUND($C{line + 1}/$B{line + 1},4)))',
                  fmts.calc("percent"))
        s.formula(line, 4,
                  f'=IFERROR(SUMIFS(TimeCost,TimeEmployee,{who},'
                  'TimeMonth,ReportMonth),"")', fmts.calc("money"))
        s.formula(line, 5,
                  '=IFERROR(SUMIFS(TimeOvertimeHours,TimeEmployee,'
                  f'{who},TimeMonth,ReportMonth),"")',
                  fmts.calc("number1"))
    row = head + LIST_ROWS + 2

    # ------------------------------------------------ what to do next --
    row = s.section(row, "What this month is telling you", LAST_COL)
    row = s.bullets(row, [
        "Collected well below invoiced means the problem is "
        "getting paid, not getting work. Start at the top of the "
        "ageing table.",
        "Billable share under 60% means the day is being eaten by "
        "driving, loading and yard time. Look at the round order "
        "before you look at the crew.",
        "Revenue per crew hour below your charge-out rate means "
        "your prices and your times disagree. The Service Catalog "
        "tab shows which service is the culprit.",
        "A win rate over 90% is not good news - it means you are "
        "the cheapest quote in town. Test a higher price on the "
        "next five.",
        "Cancelled and rained-off visits are lost capacity. If they "
        "are not rescheduled within a fortnight they are simply "
        "gone.",
    ], LAST_COL, height=30)
    row += 1

    row = s.note(
        row,
        "Every figure on this page comes from the rows you have "
        "entered. A month with visits done but no timesheets will "
        "look wonderfully profitable and tell you nothing at all.",
        LAST_COL, height=30)

    s.finish(freeze_row=4, gridlines=False, fit_width=True)
    s.protect()
    return s
````

### `landscaping_spreadsheet/sheets/annual.py`

*281 lines · 2 functions*

> `Annual Reports` - the year, and the shape of the season.
>
> Twelve months across, the things worth comparing down the side. The
> seasonal curve is the point: a landscaping business earns most of its
> money in about seven months and pays wages for twelve, and seeing
> that written down changes how you price the busy months.

| Defines | What it does |
|---------|--------------|
| `_key(n)` | The yyyy-mm key for month n of the reporting year |
| `build(book, fmts, cfg, reg)` |  |

**Module constants:** `NAME` ('Annual Reports'), `LAST_COL` (14), `LIST_ROWS` (10).

````python
"""`Annual Reports` - the year, and the shape of the season.

Twelve months across, the things worth comparing down the side. The
seasonal curve is the point: a landscaping business earns most of its
money in about seven months and pays wages for twelve, and seeing
that written down changes how you price the busy months.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from ..config import MONTH_NAMES, Config, Palette
from ..layout import Registry
from ..writer import SheetWriter

NAME = "Annual Reports"
LAST_COL = 14
LIST_ROWS = 10


def _key(n: int) -> str:
    """The yyyy-mm key for month n of the reporting year."""
    return f'TEXT(DATE(ReportYear,{n},1),"yyyy-mm")'


def build(book, fmts, cfg: Config, reg: Registry) -> SheetWriter:
    s = SheetWriter(book, fmts, cfg, reg, NAME, tab_color=Palette.deep)

    s.banner("Annual Reports",
             "Twelve months side by side - where the money came "
             "from, when it came, and what it cost to earn.",
             LAST_COL)
    s.set_col(0, 0, 26)
    for column in range(1, 13):
        s.set_col(column, column, 11)
    s.set_col(13, LAST_COL, 14)

    row = 4
    s.merge(row, 0, 0, "Reporting year", fmts.chrome("label_bold"))
    s.formula(row, 1, "=ReportYear", fmts.calc("int"))
    s.merge(row, 2, 3, "Change it on Settings & Lists",
            fmts.chrome("note"))
    s.merge(row, 4, LAST_COL, None, fmts.chrome("note"))
    s.formula(row, 4,
              '="Season: months "&SeasonStart&" to "&SeasonEnd&'
              '".  Target for the year: "&TEXT(RevenueTarget,'
              '"#,##0")', fmts.chrome("note"))
    row += 2

    # ------------------------------------------------- the month grid --
    row = s.section(row, "Month by month", LAST_COL)
    s.table_head(row, 0, [""] + list(MONTH_NAMES) + ["Year"],
                 widths=[26] + [11] * 12 + [14])
    head = row

    def line(offset: int, label: str, formula_for, style: str,
             total: str = "sum", note: str = ""):
        """One row of the grid: twelve months, then a year column."""
        r = head + offset
        s.write(r, 0, label, fmts.chrome("label_bold"))
        for n in range(1, 13):
            s.formula(r, n, "=" + formula_for(n), fmts.calc(style))
        letters = [chr(ord("B") + i) for i in range(12)]
        span = f"B{r + 1}:M{r + 1}"
        if total == "sum":
            s.formula(r, 13, f"=SUM({span})", fmts.calc(style))
        elif total == "avg":
            s.formula(r, 13, f'=IFERROR(AVERAGE({span}),"")',
                      fmts.calc(style))
        else:
            s.formula(r, 13, "=" + total, fmts.calc(style))
        del letters
        return r

    offset = 1
    line(offset, "Visits completed",
         lambda n: f'COUNTIFS(JobMonth,{_key(n)},JobIsDone,1)', "int")
    offset += 1
    line(offset, "Revenue",
         lambda n: (f'SUMIFS(JobCharge,JobMonth,{_key(n)},JobIsDone,1)'
                    f'+SUMIFS(JobExtras,JobMonth,{_key(n)},'
                    'JobIsDone,1)'), "money")
    revenue_row = head + offset
    offset += 1
    line(offset, "Labour cost",
         lambda n: f'SUMIFS(TimeCost,TimeMonth,{_key(n)})', "money")
    labour_row = head + offset
    offset += 1
    line(offset, "Materials and expenses",
         lambda n: f'SUMIFS(ExpAmount,ExpMonth,{_key(n)})', "money")
    expense_row = head + offset
    offset += 1
    line(offset, "Fuel and vehicles",
         lambda n: f'SUMIFS(VehAmount,VehMonth,{_key(n)})', "money")
    fuel_row = head + offset
    offset += 1
    line(offset, "Profit",
         lambda n: (f'SUMIFS(JobCharge,JobMonth,{_key(n)},JobIsDone,1)'
                    f'+SUMIFS(JobExtras,JobMonth,{_key(n)},JobIsDone,1)'
                    f'-SUMIFS(TimeCost,TimeMonth,{_key(n)})'
                    f'-SUMIFS(ExpAmount,ExpMonth,{_key(n)})'
                    f'-SUMIFS(VehAmount,VehMonth,{_key(n)})'),
         "money")
    profit_row = head + offset
    offset += 1
    r = head + offset
    s.write(r, 0, "Margin", fmts.chrome("label_bold"))
    for n in range(1, 13):
        col = chr(ord("B") + n - 1)
        s.formula(r, n,
                  f'=IFERROR({col}{profit_row + 1}/'
                  f'{col}{revenue_row + 1},"")', fmts.calc("percent"))
    s.formula(r, 13,
              f'=IFERROR(N{profit_row + 1}/N{revenue_row + 1},"")',
              fmts.calc("percent"))
    s.cf(f"B{r + 1}:N{r + 1}",
         {"type": "cell", "criteria": "<", "value": 0,
          "format": fmts.cf("bad")})
    offset += 1
    line(offset, "Crew hours",
         lambda n: f'SUMIFS(TimeHours,TimeMonth,{_key(n)})',
         "number1")
    hours_row = head + offset
    offset += 1
    r = head + offset
    s.write(r, 0, "Profit per crew hour", fmts.chrome("label_bold"))
    for n in range(1, 13):
        col = chr(ord("B") + n - 1)
        s.formula(r, n,
                  f'=IFERROR({col}{profit_row + 1}/'
                  f'{col}{hours_row + 1},"")', fmts.calc("money"))
    s.formula(r, 13,
              f'=IFERROR(N{profit_row + 1}/N{hours_row + 1},"")',
              fmts.calc("money"))
    offset += 1
    line(offset, "Invoiced",
         lambda n: f'SUMIFS(InvTotal,InvMonth,{_key(n)})', "money")
    offset += 1
    line(offset, "Collected",
         lambda n: f'SUMIFS(InvPaid,InvMonth,{_key(n)})', "money")
    offset += 1
    line(offset, "Quotes sent",
         lambda n: f'COUNTIFS(QuoteMonth,{_key(n)})', "int")
    offset += 1
    line(offset, "Quotes won",
         lambda n: f'COUNTIFS(QuoteMonth,{_key(n)},QuoteIsWon,1)',
         "int")
    offset += 1
    r = head + offset
    s.write(r, 0, "Share of the year", fmts.chrome("label_bold"))
    for n in range(1, 13):
        col = chr(ord("B") + n - 1)
        s.formula(r, n,
                  f'=IFERROR({col}{revenue_row + 1}/'
                  f'N{revenue_row + 1},"")', fmts.calc("percent"))
    s.formula(r, 13, '=IF(N%d=0,"",1)' % (revenue_row + 1),
              fmts.calc("percent"))
    s.cf(f"B{r + 1}:M{r + 1}",
         {"type": "data_bar", "bar_color": "#40916C"})
    del labour_row, expense_row, fuel_row
    row = head + offset + 2

    # ------------------------------------------------- the year's shape --
    row = s.section(row, "The year in one line", LAST_COL)
    s.kpi(row, 0, "Revenue",
          f'=N{revenue_row + 1}', "money")
    s.kpi(row, 2, "Profit", f'=N{profit_row + 1}', "money")
    s.kpi(row, 4, "Margin",
          f'=IFERROR(N{profit_row + 1}/N{revenue_row + 1},"")',
          "percent0")
    s.kpi(row, 6, "vs target",
          f'=IFERROR(N{revenue_row + 1}/RevenueTarget,"")',
          "percent0", note="Against the Settings target")
    s.kpi(row, 8, "Crew hours", f'=N{hours_row + 1}', "number1")
    s.kpi(row, 10, "Per crew hour",
          f'=IFERROR(N{profit_row + 1}/N{hours_row + 1},"")',
          "money")
    row = s.kpi(row, 12, "Customers", "=SUM(CustIsActive)", "int",
                span=2, note="Active on the CRM tab")
    row += 1

    # ---------------------------------------------- season comparison --
    row = s.section(row, "In season and out", LAST_COL)
    row = s.note(
        row,
        "Your season runs between the months set on Settings. The "
        "months outside it still cost you wages, insurance and yard "
        "rent, which is the argument for pricing the busy months "
        "properly rather than hoping winter pays for itself.",
        LAST_COL, height=32)
    row += 1
    in_season = ('(MONTH(JobDateNum)>=SeasonStart)*'
                 '(MONTH(JobDateNum)<=SeasonEnd)*'
                 '(YEAR(JobDateNum)=ReportYear)*JobIsDone')
    out_season = ('((MONTH(JobDateNum)<SeasonStart)+'
                  '(MONTH(JobDateNum)>SeasonEnd))*'
                  '(YEAR(JobDateNum)=ReportYear)*JobIsDone')
    s.kpi(row, 0, "In-season revenue",
          f'=SUMPRODUCT({in_season}*JobCharge)', "money")
    s.kpi(row, 2, "In-season visits",
          f'=SUMPRODUCT({in_season})', "int")
    s.kpi(row, 4, "Out-of-season revenue",
          f'=SUMPRODUCT({out_season}*JobCharge)', "money")
    s.kpi(row, 6, "Out-of-season visits",
          f'=SUMPRODUCT({out_season})', "int")
    s.kpi(row, 8, "Season share",
          f'=IFERROR(SUMPRODUCT({in_season}*JobCharge)/'
          f'N{revenue_row + 1},"")', "percent0")
    row = s.kpi(row, 10, "Recurring income",
                "=SUMPRODUCT(ConIsLive*ConAnnualValue)", "money",
                span=4,
                note="Annual value of every live contract - the part "
                     "you can plan on")
    row += 2

    # --------------------------------------------- best and worst work --
    row = s.section(row, "What earned, and what did not", LAST_COL)
    s.table_head(row, 0,
                 ["Service", "Visits", "Revenue", "Margin",
                  "Earns per hour", "Actual vs catalogue hours"],
                 widths=[30, 11, 13, 11, 14, 22])
    head = row
    for n in range(1, LIST_ROWS + 1):
        lr = head + n
        rank = f"ROW()-{head + 1}"
        base = (f'MATCH({rank},[[Service Catalog.revenue_rank]],0)')
        s.formula(lr, 0,
                  f'=IFERROR(INDEX([[Service Catalog.name]],{base}),'
                  '"")', fmts.calc("text"))
        s.formula(lr, 1,
                  '=IFERROR(INDEX([[Service Catalog.times_sold]],'
                  f'{base})*1,"")', fmts.calc("int"))
        s.formula(lr, 2,
                  '=IFERROR(INDEX([[Service Catalog.revenue]],'
                  f'{base})*1,"")', fmts.calc("money"))
        s.formula(lr, 3,
                  '=IFERROR(INDEX([[Service Catalog.margin_pct]],'
                  f'{base})*1,"")', fmts.calc("percent"))
        s.formula(lr, 4,
                  '=IFERROR(INDEX([[Service Catalog.per_hour]],'
                  f'{base})*1,"")', fmts.calc("money"))
        s.formula(lr, 5,
                  '=IFERROR(IF(INDEX('
                  f'[[Service Catalog.avg_actual]],{base})="","",'
                  'TEXT(INDEX([[Service Catalog.avg_actual]],'
                  f'{base}),"0.0")&" vs "&'
                  'TEXT(INDEX([[Service Catalog.hours_per_unit]],'
                  f'{base}),"0.0")),"")', fmts.calc("text"))
    row = head + LIST_ROWS + 2

    # --------------------------------------------------- the questions --
    row = s.section(row, "The questions a year of records can answer",
                    LAST_COL)
    row = s.bullets(row, [
        "Which month actually pays for the winter? Look at profit, "
        "not revenue - a busy June with thin margins pays for "
        "nothing.",
        "Is the recurring income growing? Contracts are the only "
        "part of a landscaping business you can plan on, and the "
        "number above is what a buyer would value.",
        "Which services earn their hours? The table above sorts by "
        "revenue; read the 'earns per hour' column instead, and "
        "then read the one next to it.",
        "Where did the catalogue times turn out to be wrong? Any "
        "service whose actual hours are consistently above the "
        "catalogue is underpriced, every single visit.",
        "How many customers were lost this year, and why? The "
        "Customer CRM and Customer Feedback tabs between them hold "
        "the answer, and it is almost never price.",
    ], LAST_COL, height=30)
    row += 1

    row = s.note(
        row,
        "This page reports the year set on the Settings tab. Change "
        "the reporting year there and every figure here moves with "
        "it, so last year's report is always one cell away.",
        LAST_COL, height=28)

    s.finish(freeze_row=4, gridlines=False, fit_width=True)
    s.protect()
    return s
````

---

## 11. The quality tools

Not shipped with the workbook, but the reason it can be trusted: a static validator that checks the Customer, Property, Job and Invoice joins in both directions and holds the seven technical decisions to account, a bracket and placeholder checker, a specification checker and a sample data checker that both run before the file is written, and a recalculation harness that really evaluates every formula.

### `tools/validate_landscaping_workbook.py`

*1,313 lines · 18 functions*

> Static checks on a generated Ultimate Landscaping & Lawn Care Business Manager.
>
> Run it with any Python that has openpyxl installed::
>
>     python tools/validate_landscaping_workbook.py output/landscaping.xlsx
>
> It never opens Excel - it parses the XML and reports anything that would
> break for a buyer: unresolved template tokens, formulas pointing at the
> wrong sheet, defined names that do not exist, missing sheet protection,
> formula cells that are not locked, input cells that are, and - the one
> that matters most here - any break in the chain of IDs that holds the
> customers, properties, quotes, jobs, contracts, crew, equipment,
> materials, invoices and profit figures together.
>
> Six things this product cannot be allowed to get wrong, and all six
> are checked below:
>
> * the four IDs connect - Customer, Property, Job and Invoice;
> * a quote is priced from lawn size, labour, materials, equipment,
>   travel and a margin, not from a number somebody guessed;
> * a job is costed twice, estimated and actual, and the gap is shown;
> * recurring contracts work out their own next visit date;
> * equipment warns on engine hours *and* on elapsed months;
> * the route planner is described as manual organisation - never as
>   GPS navigation or automatic route optimisation - and nothing in the
>   file claims to sync with accounting, payroll or booking software.

| Defines | What it does |
|---------|--------------|
| `_colnum(ref)` |  |
| `check_raw_xml(path)` | Overlapping merges and stray hyperlinks - Excel calls these corrupt |
| `paren_depth(formula)` | Open brackets minus closing ones, ignoring anything inside text |
| `_top_level(text, wanted)` | Positions of `wanted` characters outside brackets and strings |
| `_arguments(formula)` | Every comma separated argument, at every bracket depth |
| `precedence_slip(formula)` | `a+b/c` at the top level of a division - almost always a bug |
| `_span(text)` | (rows, columns) of an A1 range, or None if it is not one |
| `_calls(formula, names)` | Yield (function, [argument, ...]) for every call in *formula* |
| `ragged_criteria(formula)` | COUNTIFS/SUMIFS whose ranges are not all the same shape |
| `collapsed_criteria(formula)` | A COUNTIFS/SUMIFS criteria *range* that carries its own test |
| `_name_target(wb, name)` | The one cell a defined name points at, or None |
| `check_pages(wb, problems, notes)` | The summary pages, checked through their defined names |
| `_specs()` | Every register specification, keyed by sheet name |
| `check_chain(wb, problems, notes)` | The five workflows only hold while every one of these does |
| `check_id_joins(wb, problems, notes)` | Four IDs hold the workbook together |
| `check_decisions(wb, problems, notes)` | The decisions this product is not allowed to get wrong |
| `check_promises(wb, problems, notes)` | Four promises this product makes to the buyer in writing |
| `check(path)` |  |

**Module constants:** `FUNCTION_RE` (re.compile(...)), `NAME_RE` (re.compile(...)), `BARE_COL_RE` (re.compile(...)), `KNOWN_FUNCTIONS` ({'IF', 'IFERROR', 'SUM', 'SUMIF', 'SUMIFS', 'COUNT', 'COU...), `MODERN_ONLY` ({'XLOOKUP', 'IFS', 'MAXIFS', 'MINIFS', 'TEXTJOIN', 'CONCA...), `PRECEDENCE_OK` (0 entries), `TIME_DIVISOR_RE` (re.compile(...)), `COMPLEMENT_RE` (re.compile(...)), `DIVISOR_RE` (re.compile(...)), `RANGE_RE` (re.compile(...)), `SEEDED_INPUTS` ({'Monthly Reports!D5', 'Route Planner!C6', 'Route Planner...), `CHAIN_CHECKS` (100 items), `PAGE_FORMULAS` (4 items), `PAGE_INPUTS` (45 items) and 1 more.

````python
"""Static checks on a generated Ultimate Landscaping & Lawn Care Business Manager.

Run it with any Python that has openpyxl installed::

    python tools/validate_landscaping_workbook.py output/landscaping.xlsx

It never opens Excel - it parses the XML and reports anything that would
break for a buyer: unresolved template tokens, formulas pointing at the
wrong sheet, defined names that do not exist, missing sheet protection,
formula cells that are not locked, input cells that are, and - the one
that matters most here - any break in the chain of IDs that holds the
customers, properties, quotes, jobs, contracts, crew, equipment,
materials, invoices and profit figures together.

Six things this product cannot be allowed to get wrong, and all six
are checked below:

* the four IDs connect - Customer, Property, Job and Invoice;
* a quote is priced from lawn size, labour, materials, equipment,
  travel and a margin, not from a number somebody guessed;
* a job is costed twice, estimated and actual, and the gap is shown;
* recurring contracts work out their own next visit date;
* equipment warns on engine hours *and* on elapsed months;
* the route planner is described as manual organisation - never as
  GPS navigation or automatic route optimisation - and nothing in the
  file claims to sync with accounting, payroll or booking software.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import os
import re
import sys
import zipfile
from collections import Counter, defaultdict

try:
    import openpyxl
except ImportError:                                   # pragma: no cover
    print("openpyxl is required: pip install openpyxl")
    raise SystemExit(2)

sys.path.insert(0, os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))))
from landscaping_spreadsheet.build import (                # noqa: E402
    SHEET_ORDER as EXPECTED_SHEETS)

FUNCTION_RE = re.compile(r"\b([A-Z][A-Z0-9\.]{1,})\s*\(")
NAME_RE = re.compile(r"(?<![A-Za-z0-9_.!$\"])([A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_.]{2,})"
                     r"(?![A-Za-z0-9_.(!])")
#: a column letter with no row number - almost always a missing {r}
BARE_COL_RE = re.compile(r"(?<![A-Za-z0-9_.!$\"])([A-Z]{1,3})"
                         r"(?![A-Za-z0-9_.(!$:])")

KNOWN_FUNCTIONS = {
    "IF", "IFERROR", "SUM", "SUMIF", "SUMIFS", "COUNT", "COUNTA", "COUNTIF",
    "COUNTIFS", "AVERAGE", "AVERAGEIF", "AVERAGEIFS", "MAX", "MIN", "ROUND",
    "ROUNDUP", "ROUNDDOWN", "INDEX", "MATCH", "OFFSET", "TEXT", "VALUE",
    "LEFT", "RIGHT", "MID", "LEN", "TRIM", "UPPER", "LOWER", "PROPER",
    "CONCATENATE", "TODAY", "NOW", "DATE", "DAY", "MONTH", "YEAR", "WEEKDAY",
    "WEEKNUM", "EOMONTH", "EDATE", "DATEDIF", "DAYS", "NETWORKDAYS", "HOUR",
    "MINUTE", "TIME", "SUMPRODUCT", "LARGE", "SMALL", "RANK", "ABS", "AND",
    "OR", "NOT", "ISBLANK", "ISNUMBER", "ISTEXT", "ISERROR", "ISNA", "NA",
    "CHOOSE", "VLOOKUP", "HLOOKUP", "LOOKUP", "SUBSTITUTE", "REPT",
    "MEDIAN", "STDEV", "FLOOR", "CEILING", "INT", "MOD", "SIGN", "RAND",
    "SEARCH", "FIND", "EXACT", "N", "T", "TRUE", "FALSE", "ROW", "ROWS",
    "COLUMN", "COLUMNS", "YEARFRAC", "INDIRECT", "ADDRESS", "CELL",
    "SUBTOTAL", "TRANSPOSE", "PRODUCT", "POWER", "SQRT", "COUNTBLANK",
    "ISERR", "ISLOGICAL", "ISREF", "TYPE", "NUMBERVALUE", "DATEVALUE",
    "TIMEVALUE", "WORKDAY", "FREQUENCY", "PERCENTILE",
}

#: functions that do not exist in Excel 2010 / Google Sheets
MODERN_ONLY = {
    "XLOOKUP", "IFS", "MAXIFS", "MINIFS", "TEXTJOIN", "CONCAT", "SWITCH",
    "LET", "LAMBDA", "FILTER", "SORT", "SORTBY", "UNIQUE", "SEQUENCE",
    "TEXTSPLIT", "AGGREGATE", "IFNA",
}


def _colnum(ref: str) -> tuple:
    match = re.match(r"([A-Z]+)(\d+)", ref)
    letters, row = match.group(1), int(match.group(2))
    num = 0
    for char in letters:
        num = num * 26 + (ord(char) - 64)
    return num, row


def check_raw_xml(path: str) -> list:
    """Overlapping merges and stray hyperlinks - Excel calls these corrupt."""
    found = []
    with zipfile.ZipFile(path) as zf:
        book = zf.read("xl/workbook.xml").decode()
        sheets = re.findall(r'<sheet name="([^"]+)"[^>]*r:id="rId(\d+)"',
                            book)
        sheet_names = {name for name, _ in sheets}
        for name, rid in sheets:
            try:
                xml = zf.read(f"xl/worksheets/sheet{rid}.xml").decode()
            except KeyError:
                continue
            merges = re.findall(r'<mergeCell ref="([^"]+)"/>', xml)
            boxes = []
            for ref in merges:
                start, _, end = ref.partition(":")
                end = end or start
                c1, r1 = _colnum(start)
                c2, r2 = _colnum(end)
                boxes.append((c1, r1, c2, r2, ref))
            for i, a in enumerate(boxes):
                for b in boxes[i + 1:]:
                    if (a[0] <= b[2] and b[0] <= a[2]
                            and a[1] <= b[3] and b[1] <= a[3]):
                        found.append(f"{name}: merges overlap "
                                     f"{a[4]} / {b[4]}")
            for location in re.findall(
                    r'<hyperlink ref="[^"]+" location="([^"]+)"', xml):
                target = location.split("!")[0].strip("'").replace("''", "'")
                if target not in sheet_names:
                    found.append(f"{name}: hyperlink points at missing "
                                 f"sheet '{target}'")
            for link in re.findall(r'<hyperlink ref="([^"]+)"', xml):
                lc, lr = _colnum(link)
                for c1, r1, c2, r2, ref in boxes:
                    if (c1 <= lc <= c2 and r1 <= lr <= r2
                            and (lc, lr) != (c1, r1)):
                        found.append(f"{name}: hyperlink {link} is inside "
                                     f"merged range {ref}")
    return found


def paren_depth(formula: str) -> int:
    """Open brackets minus closing ones, ignoring anything inside text.

    Excel escapes a quote inside a string by doubling it, so `""` is not
    the end of the string. Deeply nested IF() chains built by string
    concatenation are the one bug this whole file exists to catch: Excel
    and openpyxl both accept a short formula silently, and the buyer
    sees the damage.
    """
    depth = 0
    in_text = False
    i = 0
    while i < len(formula):
        char = formula[i]
        if in_text:
            if char == '"':
                if i + 1 < len(formula) and formula[i + 1] == '"':
                    i += 1
                else:
                    in_text = False
        elif char == '"':
            in_text = True
        elif char == "(":
            depth += 1
        elif char == ")":
            depth -= 1
        i += 1
    return depth


#: divisions that really are meant to read `a + b/c`.  Each one is the
#: formula with its numbers blanked out, so one entry covers the whole
#: column.
PRECEDENCE_OK = {
    # nothing here yet: every `a + b/c` on this workbook is bracketed
    # in the source, which is the honest fix rather than a waiver
}

#: Dividing by one of these is a unit conversion, not a mis-bracket.
#: A wedding timeline is built as `ceremony_time + minutes/1440` -
#: a date serial plus a fraction of a day - and bracketing it as
#: `(ceremony_time + minutes)/1440` would be the actual bug.
TIME_DIVISOR_RE = re.compile(r"/(?:1440|86400|24|60)\b")


def _top_level(text, wanted):
    """Positions of `wanted` characters outside brackets and strings."""
    out = []
    depth = 0
    in_text = False
    i = 0
    while i < len(text):
        char = text[i]
        if in_text:
            if char == '"':
                if i + 1 < len(text) and text[i + 1] == '"':
                    i += 1
                else:
                    in_text = False
        elif char == '"':
            in_text = True
        elif char == "(":
            depth += 1
        elif char == ")":
            depth -= 1
        elif depth == 0 and char in wanted:
            before = text[i - 1] if i else ""
            if char != "/" and (before == "" or before in "(,*/+-^<>=&"):
                # a leading sign, not an operator between two values
                i += 1
                continue
            out.append(i)
        i += 1
    return out


def _arguments(formula):
    """Every comma separated argument, at every bracket depth."""
    starts = {0: 0}
    depth = 0
    in_text = False
    i = 0
    while i < len(formula):
        char = formula[i]
        if in_text:
            if char == '"':
                if i + 1 < len(formula) and formula[i + 1] == '"':
                    i += 1
                else:
                    in_text = False
        elif char == '"':
            in_text = True
        elif char == "(":
            depth += 1
            starts[depth] = i + 1
        elif char == ")":
            yield formula[starts.get(depth, 0):i]
            depth -= 1
        elif char == ",":
            yield formula[starts.get(depth, 0):i]
            starts[depth] = i + 1
        i += 1
    yield formula[starts.get(0, 0):]


#: `1-x/y` is the complement of a ratio - an on-time rate, a stock
#: accuracy score, a discount depth. `(1-x)/y` is not a thing anybody
#: means by it.
COMPLEMENT_RE = re.compile(r"(?:^|[(,=<>&*+-])\s*\d+(?:\.\d+)?$")
#: the divisor after a `/`, so `a/n + b/n` - two terms averaged over
#: the same window - can be told apart from `(a+b)/n`
DIVISOR_RE = re.compile(r"[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_.!$']*|\d+(?:\.\d+)?")


def precedence_slip(formula):
    """`a+b/c` at the top level of a division - almost always a bug."""
    body = re.sub(r'"[^"]*"', '""', formula)
    for match in re.finditer(r"/", body):
        if TIME_DIVISOR_RE.match(body[match.start():]):
            continue
        divisor = DIVISOR_RE.match(body[match.start() + 1:])
        divisor = divisor.group(0) if divisor else ""
        i, depth = match.start() - 1, 0
        while i >= 0:
            ch = body[i]
            if ch == ")":
                depth += 1
            elif ch == "(":
                if depth == 0:
                    break
                depth -= 1
            elif depth == 0 and ch in "+-" and i > 0 and \
                    body[i - 1] not in "(,*/+-<>=&":
                left = body[:i]
                if ch == "-" and COMPLEMENT_RE.search(left):
                    break
                if ch == "+" and divisor and \
                        left.rstrip().endswith("/" + divisor):
                    break
                return body[max(0, i - 25):match.start() + 12]
            i -= 1
    return ""


RANGE_RE = re.compile(
    r"^(?:(?:'[^']+'|[A-Za-z0-9_.]+)!)?"
    r"\$?([A-Z]{1,3})\$?(\d+):\$?([A-Z]{1,3})\$?(\d+)$")


def _span(text: str):
    """(rows, columns) of an A1 range, or None if it is not one."""
    match = RANGE_RE.match(text.strip())
    if not match:
        return None
    c1, r1, c2, r2 = match.groups()

    def num(letters):
        value = 0
        for char in letters:
            value = value * 26 + (ord(char) - 64)
        return value
    return (abs(int(r2) - int(r1)) + 1, abs(num(c2) - num(c1)) + 1)


def _calls(formula: str, names):
    """Yield (function, [argument, ...]) for every call in *formula*."""
    depth = 0
    in_text = False
    stack = []
    i = 0
    while i < len(formula):
        char = formula[i]
        if in_text:
            if char == '"':
                if i + 1 < len(formula) and formula[i + 1] == '"':
                    i += 1
                else:
                    in_text = False
        elif char == '"':
            in_text = True
        elif char == "(":
            head = re.search(r"([A-Z][A-Z0-9\.]*)$", formula[:i])
            stack.append([head.group(1) if head else "", i + 1, [], depth])
            depth += 1
        elif char == ")":
            depth -= 1
            if stack:
                name, start, args, _ = stack.pop()
                args.append(formula[start:i])
                if name in names:
                    yield name, args
                if stack:
                    stack[-1][1] = stack[-1][1]
        elif char == "," and stack and depth == stack[-1][3] + 1:
            frame = stack[-1]
            frame[2].append(formula[frame[1]:i])
            frame[1] = i + 1
        i += 1


def ragged_criteria(formula: str) -> str:
    """COUNTIFS/SUMIFS whose ranges are not all the same shape.

    Excel answers #VALUE! the moment two criteria ranges differ in
    size, and it is an easy mistake to make when one of them is a
    running range that grows row by row.
    """
    for name, args in _calls(formula, ("COUNTIFS", "SUMIFS",
                                       "AVERAGEIFS")):
        first = 1 if name in ("SUMIFS", "AVERAGEIFS") else 0
        ranges = [args[0]] if first else []
        ranges += args[first::2]
        spans = {}
        for text in ranges:
            span = _span(text)
            if span:
                spans.setdefault(span, text)
        if len(spans) > 1:
            return f"{name}: " + " vs ".join(sorted(spans.values()))
    return ""


def collapsed_criteria(formula: str) -> str:
    """A COUNTIFS/SUMIFS criteria *range* that carries its own test.

    A criterion is two arguments - the range, then the thing it is
    compared against.  Writing it as one argument::

        COUNTIFS(prop, "PRP-01", scores="Yes")

    leaves the function an odd number of arguments and makes Excel
    answer #VALUE! on every row.  It reads perfectly naturally, which
    is exactly why it is worth a check: `SUMPRODUCT((range="Yes")*..)`
    really is written that way, so the habit carries over.
    """
    for name, args in _calls(formula, ("COUNTIFS", "SUMIFS",
                                       "AVERAGEIFS")):
        first = 1 if name in ("SUMIFS", "AVERAGEIFS") else 0
        if (len(args) - first) % 2:
            return f"{name}: odd number of range/criteria arguments"
        for text in args[first::2]:
            stripped = re.sub(r'"[^"]*"', '""', text)
            if re.search(r"[<>=]", stripped):
                return f"{name}: criteria range carries a test -> {text}"
    return ""


#: input cells that ship with a formula in them as a helpful default -
#: the buyer is meant to be able to type a fixed value straight over it

#: value cells that are deliberately left unlocked even though a
#: formula sits in them - the buyer is meant to type over the top
SEEDED_INPUTS = {
    "Monthly Reports!D5",                 # the month being reported on
````
